    -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    INTELLIGENCE & ANALYSIS LAYER
    -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    
        PURPOSE
        -------
        The Intelligence and Analytical Layer converts operational records generated by the
        IOTS into measurable performance indicators, workload insights, attendance patterns,
        exceptions, risks, trends, and management reports.
    
        This layer does not directly perform day-to-day work operations.
        Instead, it analyzes the records created by the Management Layer and presents
        meaningful information to Employees, Managers, and higher-level management according
        to their authorized scope.
    
        The layer should support:
        - Historical analysis
        - Current operational analysis
        - Trend identification
        - Performance measurement
        - Workload analysis
        - Attendance analysis
        - Exception detection
        - Risk classification
        - Management reporting
        - Role-based visibility
        - Drill-down from summary information to underlying records
    
    
        1. PERFORMANCE ANALYTICS
        ========================
    
        FUNCTION
        --------
        Performance Analytics measures how effectively an individual employee, manager,
        team, role, or organizational unit is completing assigned work against defined
        expectations.
    
        The system should not rely on a single performance value. It should combine
        multiple measurable indicators so that performance represents actual work outcomes
        rather than only task volume.
    
    
        1.1 TASK COMPLETION RATE
        ------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures the percentage of assigned tasks that were completed within the selected
        analysis period.
    
        Logic:
    
        Completion Rate =
        (Number of tasks completed / Number of tasks that were due or assigned for the
        selected measurement period) × 100
    
        Handling Logic:
        - Identify all tasks belonging to the selected employee, manager, team, role, or
          organizational scope.
        - Determine which tasks fall inside the selected reporting period.
        - Count tasks whose final workflow outcome is Completed / Closed.
        - Count tasks that were expected to be completed during that period.
        - Exclude cancelled tasks from completion calculations where cancellation is an
          administrative outcome rather than employee non-completion.
        - Reassigned tasks should retain their history and ownership information.
        - Reassignment must not create duplicate task counts.
        - Display the result together with the total number of tasks used in the calculation.
        - Allow the user to drill from the percentage into the underlying task records.
    
        Possible Output:
        - Completion Rate: 91%
        - Completed: 41
        - Expected: 45
        - Remaining: 4
    
    
        1.2 ON-TIME COMPLETION
        ----------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures how many completed tasks were completed on or before their applicable
        deadline.
    
        Logic:
        - Compare the actual completion/approval timestamp against the task deadline.
        - A task completed on or before the deadline is classified as On-Time.
        - A task completed after the deadline is classified as Late.
        - Tasks whose deadlines were officially extended should use the latest authorized
          deadline for the final comparison, while retaining the original deadline in
          historical records.
        - Tasks that became overdue and were later completed remain visible as late
          completions.
        - Display both on-time percentage and late-completion count.
    
        Output:
        - On-Time Completion Rate
        - Late Completion Rate
        - Number of tasks completed on time
        - Number of tasks completed late
        - Average delay duration for late tasks
    
    
        1.3 AVERAGE COMPLETION TIME
        ---------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures the average time required to move tasks from active execution to
        completion.
    
        Logic:
        - Determine the defined start point for the task.
        - Determine the actual completion point.
        - Calculate the elapsed completion duration.
        - Where the workflow records blocked/waiting periods, the reporting logic may
          distinguish total elapsed time from active work time.
        - Use a consistent business definition throughout the organization.
        - Do not mix gross elapsed duration and active work duration without explicitly
          labelling them.
        - Display the average together with task volume because a small sample can distort
          interpretation.
    
        Output:
        - Average completion time
        - Median completion time where supported
        - Fastest completion
        - Longest completion
        - Comparison with previous period
    
    
        1.4 TARGET-BASED PERFORMANCE
        ----------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures actual achievement against a defined target.
    
        Examples:
        - Number of tasks expected
        - Tasks completed
        - Daily target
        - Weekly target
        - Monthly target
        - Role-specific operational target
    
        Logic:
        - Retrieve the target applicable to the selected employee, role, team, or period.
        - Determine actual achieved value.
        - Compare actual achievement against target.
        - Calculate achievement percentage.
    
        Target Achievement =
        (Actual achievement / Defined target) × 100
    
        Handling:
        - Targets should be period-specific.
        - Targets should be role-aware where different roles have different expectations.
        - Changes to targets should preserve their historical versions.
        - Reports should indicate the target used for the calculation.
        - A target of zero must not produce an invalid division result.
        - Where no target exists, show "Target Not Configured" instead of assuming zero.
    
        Output:
        - Target
        - Actual
        - Achievement %
        - Variance from Target
        - Above / On Target / Below Target
    
    
        1.5 EMPLOYEE-SPECIFIC PERFORMANCE
        ----------------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Provides an individual employee performance view.
    
        Logic:
        - Filter all permitted task, attendance, activity, and performance records for the
          selected employee.
        - Combine relevant indicators.
        - Show performance over the selected period.
        - Compare the employee with their own historical performance.
        - Where organizational policy permits, compare against applicable team or role
          benchmarks.
        - Do not expose information outside the viewer's authorized scope.
    
        Possible Indicators:
        - Task completion rate
        - On-time completion
        - Average completion time
        - Target achievement
        - Overdue task count
        - Reassignment frequency
        - Blocker frequency
        - Work activity consistency
        - Attendance indicators
    
    
        1.6 MANAGER-SPECIFIC PERFORMANCE
        --------------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures managerial effectiveness based on team-level operational outcomes.
    
        Logic:
        - Aggregate performance of employees under the manager's authorized scope.
        - Measure task allocation effectiveness.
        - Measure overdue and blocked work.
        - Measure review and approval activity.
        - Measure resolution of employee issues.
        - Measure team target achievement.
        - Compare current team performance with previous periods.
        - Do not evaluate the manager solely from employee task completion count.
    
        Possible Indicators:
        - Team completion rate
        - Team on-time completion
        - Overdue workload
        - Review turnaround time
        - Reassignment frequency
        - Issue resolution time
        - Team target achievement
        - Team workload balance
    
    
        1.7 TEAM PERFORMANCE
        ---------------------
    
        Definition:
        Provides an aggregated performance view for a team.
    
        Logic:
        - Group employee records by team.
        - Aggregate task and performance indicators.
        - Show distribution rather than only a single team average.
        - Identify whether poor team results originate from a few employees or are broadly
          distributed.
        - Allow drill-down:
          Team → Employee → Task → Activity / Evidence
    
    
        1.8 ROLE-BASED PERFORMANCE
        --------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures performance according to the work type and expectations applicable to
        a specific role.
    
        Logic:
        - Identify the user's authorized role/designation.
        - Apply the performance metrics configured for that role.
        - Avoid forcing identical KPIs onto roles with fundamentally different work.
        - Preserve a common reporting structure while allowing role-specific measures.
    
        Example:
        A BDE may be measured using lead/task outcomes,
        while a Graphic Designer may be measured using creative-task completion,
        revision cycles, submission quality, and deadline adherence.
    
    
        2. TASK & WORKLOAD ANALYTICS
        ============================
    
        FUNCTION
        --------
        Task and Workload Analytics analyzes how work is distributed, how much work is
        pending, where bottlenecks exist, who is overloaded, and how effectively assigned
        work is progressing.
    
        The layer should distinguish between:
        - Task quantity
        - Task status
        - Workload
        - Work progress
        - Task ownership
        - Reassignment history
    
    
        2.1 TASK DISTRIBUTION
        ---------------------
    
        Definition:
        Shows how tasks are distributed across employees, teams, managers, roles,
        priorities, and statuses.
    
        Logic:
        - Group tasks by the selected dimension.
        - Count tasks in each group.
        - Allow filtering by:
          - Date
          - Employee
          - Manager
          - Team
          - Role
          - Priority
          - Status
        - Show both task count and percentage of total.
        - Permit drill-down into the underlying task list.
    
        Example:
        Team A = 40%
        Team B = 35%
        Team C = 25%
    
    
        2.2 PENDING TASK ANALYSIS
        -------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures work that remains incomplete.
    
        Logic:
        - Identify tasks that are not in a final completed/closed state.
        - Separate normal active work from exceptions such as overdue or blocked work.
        - Group pending tasks by employee, team, priority, age, and deadline.
        - Highlight long-pending tasks.
        - Allow drill-down to task details.
    
    
        2.3 OVERDUE TASK ANALYSIS
        -------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Identifies tasks whose applicable deadline has passed without completion.
    
        Logic:
        - Compare current/reporting timestamp with the current authorized task deadline.
        - If the task remains incomplete after the deadline, classify it as Overdue.
        - Overdue tasks should not be treated merely as ordinary pending work.
        - Record:
          - Overdue date
          - Duration overdue
          - Priority
          - Current owner
          - Manager
          - Whether the task was reassigned
          - Whether a manager intervention occurred
        - Sort or classify overdue tasks by severity.
        - Provide a direct path from analytics to manager intervention.
    
    
        2.4 REASSIGNMENT FREQUENCY
        --------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures how frequently tasks are reassigned.
    
        Logic:
        - Count reassignment events from the task history.
        - Do not count a reassigned task as multiple tasks.
        - Count each actual ownership-transfer event separately.
        - Calculate:
          - Total reassignment events
          - Number of tasks reassigned
          - Reassignments per task
          - Reassignment by employee/team/manager
        - Identify repeated reassignment patterns.
        - Preserve the reason and history of every reassignment.
    
        This can identify:
        - Capacity problems
        - Assignment problems
        - Unclear ownership
        - Workflow problems
        - Repeated operational intervention
    
    
        2.5 PARTICULAR-EMPLOYEE WORKLOAD
        -------------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures the amount and intensity of work assigned to a specific employee.
    
        Logic:
        - Retrieve all tasks currently assigned to the employee.
        - Categorize by:
          - Active
          - Pending
          - Overdue
          - High priority
          - Blocked
          - Waiting for review
        - Consider deadlines and task priority rather than simply counting tasks.
        - Show current workload and historical workload.
        - Compare current workload against the employee's configured/expected capacity,
          where such capacity information exists.
    
        Possible Output:
        Employee
        Current Tasks
        High Priority Tasks
        Overdue Tasks
        Blocked Tasks
        Upcoming Deadlines
        Workload Level
    
    
        2.6 TEAM WORKLOAD
        -----------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures the distribution of active work across the team.
    
        Logic:
        - Aggregate employee workload records.
        - Compare workload across team members.
        - Identify concentration of work on individual employees.
        - Identify employees with significantly lower or higher active workload.
        - Show task volume alongside deadline pressure.
        - Provide a manager drill-down from:
          Team → Employee → Tasks
    
    
        2.7 WORKLOAD BALANCE
        --------------------
    
        Definition:
        Determines whether assigned work is reasonably distributed.
    
        Logic:
        - Calculate workload indicators for all applicable employees.
        - Compare workload against configured capacity or expected workload.
        - Classify employees as:
          - Normal
          - High
          - Overloaded
          where the actual thresholds are configurable.
        - Generate management attention when workload becomes materially unbalanced.
        - Do not automatically reassign work solely because an analytical threshold is
          reached; analytics should identify the condition, while authorized management
          actions perform the reassignment.
    
    
        2.8 UPCOMING WORKLOAD
        ---------------------
    
        Definition:
        Predicts near-term workload pressure from tasks whose deadlines are approaching.
    
        Logic:
        - Identify upcoming deadlines.
        - Group tasks by employee/team.
        - Consider task priority and due-date proximity.
        - Detect concentration of deadlines within a short period.
        - Flag employees likely to become overloaded.
        - Present this as an operational warning rather than a confirmed failure.
    
    
        3. ATTENDANCE ANALYTICS
        =======================
    
        FUNCTION
        --------
        Attendance Analytics analyzes attendance records, working hours, late arrivals,
        absences, availability, and workforce attendance patterns.
    
        Attendance and Availability must remain separate concepts.
    
        Attendance answers:
        "Was the employee present / working?"
    
        Availability answers:
        "Can the employee currently accept or handle work?"
    
    
        3.1 LATE ARRIVAL TRENDS
        -----------------------
    
        Definition:
        Identifies patterns in late arrivals over time.
    
        Logic:
        - Retrieve attendance check-in records.
        - Determine whether each arrival was on time or late using the organization's
          configured working schedule and applicable grace period.
        - Count late events by:
          - Employee
          - Team
          - Role
          - Date
          - Week
          - Month
        - Show trend over time.
        - Distinguish occasional late arrivals from repeated patterns.
        - Link back to attendance records for verification.
    
    
        3.2 ABSENCE TRENDS
        ------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures absence patterns across employees and teams.
    
        Logic:
        - Identify attendance records classified as absent.
        - Group by employee, team, role, and reporting period.
        - Distinguish approved leave from unexplained/flagged absence where the system
          contains that information.
        - Show frequency and trend.
        - Do not treat approved leave automatically as poor performance.
    
    
        3.3 AVAILABILITY PATTERNS
        -------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures how employee availability changes throughout working periods.
    
        Logic:
        - Analyze recorded availability states such as:
          Available
          Busy
          In Meeting
          On Break
          In Training
          Do Not Disturb
          Working Remotely
          On Leave
          Absent
          Offline
        - Measure duration/frequency where such timestamps are available.
        - Identify recurring periods of low availability.
        - Compare availability with workload and task deadlines.
    
    
        3.4 WORKFORCE ATTENDANCE PATTERNS
        ----------------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Provides an aggregated view of workforce attendance behavior.
    
        Logic:
        - Aggregate attendance records across teams, roles, branches, or departments.
        - Identify:
          - Attendance rate
          - Late-arrival patterns
          - Absence patterns
          - Remote/off-site patterns where recorded
          - Working-hour trends
        - Compare current period with historical periods.
        - Identify recurring organization-level patterns.
    
    
        3.5 WORK HOURS ANALYSIS
        -----------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures recorded working duration for employees and teams.
    
        Logic:
        - Use check-in and check-out records.
        - Account for recorded break periods where supported.
        - Calculate work duration according to the organization's configured attendance
          policy.
        - Show:
          - Total hours
          - Average hours
          - Daily hours
          - Team totals
        - Flag incomplete or inconsistent attendance records separately instead of
          silently treating them as zero hours.
    
    
        4. EXCEPTION / RISK ANALYSIS
        ============================
    
        FUNCTION
        --------
        Exception and Risk Analysis identifies operational conditions that require
        attention.
    
        It converts raw operational conditions into manageable warnings.
    
        The system should distinguish between:
        - Normal operation
        - Attention required
        - High-risk operational condition
    
        Risk classification should be rule-based and configurable unless a formal
        predictive model is implemented.
    
    
        4.1 OVERDUE TASK DETECTION
        --------------------------
    
        Logic:
        - Continuously or periodically evaluate active task deadlines.
        - Detect tasks whose deadline has passed without completion.
        - Calculate overdue duration.
        - Consider task priority.
        - Consider whether the task is blocked, awaiting review, or otherwise subject
          to a valid workflow condition.
        - Create an analytical exception where appropriate.
        - Notify or surface the exception to the authorized manager dashboard.
        - Track whether the exception was resolved, extended, reassigned, or escalated.
    
    
        4.2 WORKLOAD RISK
        -----------------
    
        Definition:
        Identifies situations where an employee/team may not have sufficient capacity
        to handle assigned work within expected deadlines.
    
        Logic:
        - Evaluate active task count.
        - Evaluate upcoming deadlines.
        - Evaluate priority.
        - Evaluate overdue tasks.
        - Evaluate blocked work.
        - Compare workload against configured capacity where available.
        - Combine the conditions into a workload-risk assessment.
    
        Example logic:
        High active workload
        +
        multiple approaching deadlines
        +
        existing overdue work
        =
        Higher workload risk
    
    
        4.3 REPEATED REASSIGNMENT RISK
        ------------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Detects when a task or employee is repeatedly involved in reassignment events.
    
        Logic:
        - Count reassignment events over a configurable period.
        - Determine whether the configured threshold has been exceeded.
        - Identify:
          - Task repeatedly reassigned
          - Employee frequently receiving reassigned tasks
          - Employee frequently losing assigned tasks
          - Team-level reassignment concentration
        - Record the reason wherever available.
        - Create a risk/attention indicator when the configured threshold is reached.
        - Do not interpret reassignment automatically as employee failure; the reason
          must remain available for management review.
    
    
        4.4 RISK CLASSIFICATION
        -----------------------
    
        The system can classify detected exceptions into:
    
        LOW
        ---
        Indicates a condition that should be monitored but does not currently require
        urgent intervention.
    
        MEDIUM
        ------
        Indicates a recurring or materially important condition that should receive
        manager attention.
    
        HIGH
        ----
        Indicates a condition that requires immediate managerial or authorized higher-level
        attention.
    
        Handling Logic:
        - Risk level is generated from configured business rules.
        - Rules may consider:
          - Severity
          - Frequency
          - Duration
          - Priority
          - Impact
          - Repeated occurrence
        - Risk thresholds should be configurable.
        - Every risk result should show why it was classified at that level.
        - Risk should be traceable to underlying operational records.
        - Risk status should be capable of moving through:
          Detected → Reviewed → Action Taken → Resolved
        - Resolved risks remain in historical records for trend analysis.
    
    
        4.5 PENDING REQUEST RISK
        ------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Identifies requests that remain undecided beyond the expected handling period.
    
        Examples:
        - Leave request
        - Permission request
        - Resource request
        - Task clarification request
    
        Logic:
        - Determine request creation time.
        - Determine expected decision/response period where configured.
        - Identify requests that remain pending beyond that period.
        - Group by request type, requester, manager, and age.
        - Surface delayed requests to the appropriate authority.
        - Preserve request history and decisions.
    
    
        4.6 UNRESOLVED BLOCKER ANALYSIS
        -------------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Identifies employee-reported issues/blockers that remain unresolved and may
        impact work completion.
    
        Logic:
        - Detect active blocker/issue records.
        - Measure blocker age.
        - Identify affected task.
        - Identify employee and responsible manager.
        - Determine severity.
        - Determine whether escalation has occurred.
        - Prioritize blockers based on severity, task priority, and duration.
        - Surface long-running blockers as management exceptions.
    
    
        4.7 COMBINED OPERATIONAL RISK
        -----------------------------
    
        The system may combine multiple independent exceptions to identify a larger
        operational concern.
    
        Example:
    
        Employee has:
        - High workload
        - Multiple approaching deadlines
        - Existing overdue task
        - Open blocker
    
        The analytical layer can combine those conditions into a higher-priority
        management attention item.
    
        Important:
        This does not automatically mean the employee is underperforming.
        The system identifies the operational condition and allows management to inspect
        the underlying causes.
    
    
        5. TREND & HISTORICAL ANALYSIS
        ==============================
    
        FUNCTION
        --------
        Historical analysis compares current operational performance with previous periods
        to identify improvement, deterioration, recurring patterns, and changes.
    
        Logic:
        - Store historical analytical snapshots or calculate from historical operational
          records.
        - Compare:
          - Current day vs previous day
          - Current week vs previous week
          - Current month vs previous month
          - Current period vs equivalent previous period
        - Show direction:
          - Improving
          - Stable
          - Declining
        - Allow drill-down into the records causing the trend.
    
    
        5.1 PERFORMANCE TRENDS
        ----------------------
    
        Analyze:
        - Completion rate over time
        - On-time completion over time
        - Target achievement over time
        - Average completion time over time
    
    
        5.2 WORKLOAD TRENDS
        -------------------
    
        Analyze:
        - Task volume over time
        - Pending workload
        - Overdue workload
        - Reassignment frequency
        - Workload concentration
    
    
        5.3 ATTENDANCE TRENDS
        ---------------------
    
        Analyze:
        - Attendance rate
        - Late-arrival frequency
        - Absence frequency
        - Availability patterns
        - Working-hour trends
    
    
        5.4 RISK TRENDS
        ---------------
    
        Analyze:
        - Number of detected risks
        - Low / Medium / High distribution
        - Repeated risk types
        - Average resolution time
        - Unresolved exception count
        - Recurrent operational problems
    
    
        6. REPORTING AND WORK PROGRESS REPORTS
        ======================================
    
        FUNCTION
        --------
        The Intelligence and Analytical Layer converts the available analytical data into
        structured work-progress reports for different reporting periods and authorized
        roles.
    
    
        6.1 DAILY REPORT
        ---------------
    
        Purpose:
        Provides a snapshot of current operational activity.
    
        Should include, where applicable:
        - Tasks assigned
        - Tasks completed
        - Pending tasks
        - Overdue tasks
        - Active workload
        - Attendance status
        - Significant blockers
        - Requests pending
        - Major exceptions
        - Daily performance indicators
    
        Daily reports should emphasize:
        "What happened today?"
        and
        "What requires attention now?"
    
    
        6.2 WEEKLY REPORT
        -----------------
    
        Purpose:
        Shows operational progress across the week and identifies patterns that may not
        be visible in a single day.
    
        Should include:
        - Weekly task completion
        - On-time completion
        - Target achievement
        - Workload distribution
        - Overdue work
        - Reassignment frequency
        - Attendance trends
        - Open blockers
        - Exception/risk summary
        - Performance movement
    
        Weekly reports should answer:
        "How did the team perform this week?"
        and
        "Where are recurring issues appearing?"
    
    
        6.3 MONTHLY REPORT
        ------------------
    
        Purpose:
        Provides a broader management-level view of operational performance.
    
        Should include:
        - Monthly completion rate
        - Target achievement
        - On-time performance
        - Average completion time
        - Workload trends
        - Attendance trends
        - Performance trends
        - Reassignment trends
        - Risk and exception summary
        - Resolved vs unresolved issues
        - Comparison with previous months
    
        Monthly reporting should emphasize:
        "What is the overall operational trend?"
        and
        "Where does management need to act?"
    
    
        6.4 ROLE-BASED REPORTS
        ---------------------
    
        Definition:
        Reports must change according to the role and authority of the viewer.
    
        Employee:
        - Personal task performance
        - Personal workload
        - Personal attendance
        - Personal activity
        - Personal feedback
        - Authorized personal analytics
    
        Manager:
        - Team performance
        - Team workload
        - Employee-level performance within scope
        - Attendance
        - Overdue work
        - Blockers
        - Requests
        - Exceptions
        - Team trends
    
        Higher Management:
        - Organization / branch / department performance
        - Cross-team comparisons
        - Major operational exceptions
        - Workload trends
        - Performance trends
        - High-risk conditions
        - Management-level summaries
    
        Admin:
        - System-oriented operational reporting
        - User/account activity where authorized
        - Attendance/location/system-related reporting
        - Task/system oversight
        - Audit and security reporting according to permission scope
    
    
        6.5 TEAM ANALYTICS
        ------------------
    
        Purpose:
        Provide an aggregated team-level analytical view.
    
        Should support:
        - Team task completion
        - Team on-time completion
        - Team target achievement
        - Team workload
        - Employee workload distribution
        - Team attendance
        - Team availability
        - Overdue workload
        - Reassignment frequency
        - Open blockers
        - Team risk indicators
    
        Drill-down:
        Team
        → Employee
        → Task
        → Work Activity
        → Evidence / History
    
        The same hierarchy should preserve authorization at every level.
    
    
        7. ANALYTICAL FILTERING AND DRILL-DOWN
        ======================================
    
        The analytical layer should allow users to filter information according to their
        authorized scope.
    
        Possible filters:
        - Date
        - Date range
        - Employee
        - Manager
        - Team
        - Role
        - Department / Branch where configured
        - Task priority
        - Task status
        - Request type
        - Risk level
        - Attendance status
    
        Drill-down logic:
    
        Summary Metric
            ↓
        Category / Group
            ↓
        Specific Employee / Team
            ↓
        Specific Task / Request / Attendance Record
            ↓
        Detailed Operational History
    
        Example:
    
        Team Completion Rate
            ↓
        Team A
            ↓
        Employee X
            ↓
        Task 1045
            ↓
        Task Activity / Submission / Review / Audit History
    
    
        8. DATA HANDLING LOGIC
        ======================
    
        The analytical layer should read from operational records rather than manually
        re-entering information.
    
        Primary analytical sources include:
        - User records
        - Role and authorization records
        - Task records
        - Task state history
        - Work activity records
        - Work notes
        - Effort/time records
        - Supporting documents/evidence
        - Attendance records
        - Availability records
        - Requests
        - Approval records
        - Blockers/issues
        - Escalation records
        - Reassignment history
        - Audit records
        - Configured targets
        - Configured working schedules
    
    
        8.1 STATE-AWARE ANALYSIS
        ------------------------
    
        The analytical layer must understand the difference between task states.
    
        Examples:
        Assigned
        Pending / In Progress
        Overdue
        Reassigned
        Completed
    
        Operational execution may additionally contain:
        Blocked
        Waiting for Review
        Under Review
        Changes Requested
        Issue Resolved
        Done / Closed
    
        Reassigned should be treated as a workflow event/history rather than counting the
        same task as a new task.
    
        Overdue should be treated as an exception requiring attention rather than simply
        another normal task category.
    
    
        8.2 HISTORICAL PRESERVATION
        ---------------------------
    
        Analytical results must be traceable to historical operational records.
    
        When task assignments, deadlines, priorities, roles, or other operational values
        change:
        - Current values should represent the current state.
        - Historical values should remain available for analysis.
        - Reports should be able to explain why historical results changed.
        - Reassignment, deadline extension, review, and approval history should not be
          destroyed.
    
    
        8.3 AUDIT TRACEABILITY
        ----------------------
    
        Every important analytical result should be traceable back to its source data.
    
        Example:
    
        Risk = HIGH
            ↓
        Reason:
            - 3 overdue tasks
            - 2 high-priority deadlines approaching
            - 1 unresolved blocker
            ↓
        Underlying Records:
            Task IDs
            Blocker ID
            Workload Records
            Attendance / Availability Records where relevant
    
        This allows management to verify the analytical conclusion rather than treating
        the dashboard value as unexplained.
    
    
        9. MANAGEMENT ATTENTION / ACTION CENTRE
        =======================================
    
        The analytical layer may provide an "Operational Attention" area that highlights
        items requiring human review.
    
        Possible attention items:
        - High-risk workload
        - Overdue critical tasks
        - Repeated reassignment
        - Long-pending requests
        - Unresolved blockers
        - Attendance anomalies
        - Declining performance trend
        - Significant target variance
    
        Each item should provide:
        - What happened
        - Who/what is affected
        - Why it was flagged
        - Risk level
        - When it occurred
        - Current status
        - Authorized next action
        - Link to underlying record
    
    
        10. INTELLIGENCE MATURITY
        =========================
    
        The Intelligence and Analytical Layer can be implemented in progressive levels.
    
        LEVEL 1 — DESCRIPTIVE ANALYTICS
        --------------------------------
        Answers:
        "What happened?"
    
        Examples:
        - 40 tasks completed
        - 5 tasks overdue
        - 3 requests pending
        - Attendance rate = 94%
    
    
        LEVEL 2 — DIAGNOSTIC ANALYTICS
        ------------------------------
        Answers:
        "Why is it happening?"
    
        Examples:
        - Overdue work concentrated in one employee
        - Reassignment increased during the last week
        - Delays are associated with unresolved blockers
        - Team workload is unevenly distributed
    
    
        LEVEL 3 — PREDICTIVE / ADVANCED ANALYTICS
        ------------------------------------------
        Answers:
        "What is likely to happen?"
    
        Examples:
        - Employee may become overloaded
        - Deadline risk is increasing
        - Repeated blockers may affect completion
        - Current workload may create a future overdue condition
    
        Predictive functionality should only be labelled as predictive/AI when an actual
        predictive model or appropriate analytical method is implemented.
    
        Rule-based thresholds and condition checks should remain clearly identified as
        rule-based analytics.
    
    
        11. ROLE-BASED ANALYTICAL VISIBILITY
        ====================================
    
        EMPLOYEE
        --------
        Focus:
        - My performance
        - My tasks
        - My workload
        - My attendance
        - My progress
        - My activity
        - My authorized reports
    
    
        MANAGER
        --------
        Focus:
        - Team performance
        - Team workload
        - Employee workload
        - Overdue work
        - Attendance
        - Availability
        - Blockers
        - Requests
        - Exceptions
        - Team trends
    
    
        SENIOR / GENERAL MANAGEMENT
        ---------------------------
        Focus:
        - Cross-team performance
        - Operational workload
        - Major exceptions
        - Organizational trends
        - Performance movement
        - Targets
        - Management attention areas
    
    
        CHAIRMAN / EXECUTIVE VIEW
        -------------------------
        Focus:
        - Overall operational health
        - Key performance indicators
        - Major risks
        - Major escalations
        - Team / organizational trends
        - Strategic operational summaries
    
        The analytical layer should not expose lower-level information merely because a
        person can see a dashboard. Visibility must follow authorization and data scope.
    
    
        12. FINAL ANALYTICAL OUTPUT
        ===========================
    
        The Intelligence and Analytical Layer should ultimately transform:
    
        RAW OPERATIONAL DATA
                ↓
        DATA AGGREGATION
                ↓
        METRIC CALCULATION
                ↓
        TREND ANALYSIS
                ↓
        EXCEPTION DETECTION
                ↓
        RISK CLASSIFICATION
                ↓
        MANAGEMENT INSIGHT
                ↓
        REPORT
                ↓
        MANAGEMENT ACTION
    
    
        CORE ANALYTICAL FUNCTIONS
        -------------------------
    
        1. Performance Analytics
           - Task completion rate
           - On-time completion
           - Average completion time
           - Target achievement
           - Employee-specific performance
           - Manager-specific performance
           - Team performance
           - Role-based performance
    
        2. Task & Workload Analytics
           - Task distribution
           - Pending task analysis
           - Overdue task analysis
           - Reassignment frequency
           - Employee workload
           - Team workload
           - Workload balance
           - Upcoming workload pressure
    
        3. Attendance Analytics
           - Late arrival trends
           - Absence trends
           - Availability patterns
           - Workforce attendance patterns
           - Working-hour analysis
    
        4. Exception / Risk Analysis
           - Overdue detection
           - Workload risk
           - Reassignment risk
           - Low / Medium / High risk classification
           - Pending request risk
           - Unresolved blocker analysis
           - Combined operational risk
    
        5. Trend & Historical Analysis
           - Performance trends
           - Workload trends
           - Attendance trends
           - Risk trends
           - Period-over-period comparison
    
        6. Reporting
           - Daily reports
           - Weekly reports
           - Monthly reports
           - Role-based reports
           - Team analytics
           - Management-level reports
           - Drill-down operational reports
    
        7. Management Attention
           - Identify issues
           - Explain why they were flagged
           - Classify severity/risk
           - Link to underlying records
           - Present authorized management actions
    
    
        FINAL PRINCIPLE
        ---------------
    
        The Intelligence and Analytical Layer should not merely answer:
    
        "How many tasks are there?"
    
        It should progressively answer:
    
        "What happened?"
        "How well are we performing?"
        "Where is work concentrated?"
        "Where are delays occurring?"
        "Why are delays occurring?"
        "What is becoming risky?"
        "What requires management attention?"
        "What is changing over time?"
        "What should the authorized decision-maker review or act on?"
    
        CORE LOGIC OF THIS LAYER:
    
        Task / Work / Attendance / Requests / Issues / Audit
                            ↓
                  Intelligence Engine
                            ↓
                Metrics + Trends + Exceptions
                            ↓
                    Risk / Insight
                            ↓
                  Daily / Weekly / Monthly
                        Reporting
                            ↓
                   Management Decision

    Check, in order — a missing PATH entry or a non-`C:` install drive is normal, not a sign that draw.io is missing:
    
    1. **PATH**: `where.exe draw.io` (note the dot — the executable is `draw.io.exe`, not `drawio.exe`)
    2. **Default install paths**: `C:\Program Files\draw.io\draw.io.exe`, then the per-user install `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\draw.io\draw.io.exe`
    3. **The registry** uninstall keys, which record the directory the user picked
    4. **The other drives**: `Program Files\draw.io\draw.io.exe` on `D:`, `E:`, …
    
    Save this as `find-drawio.ps1` to run the whole chain at once — it prints the first executable it finds, and nothing at all if draw.io really is not installed:
    
    ```powershell
    $c = @()
    $c += (where.exe draw.io 2>$null)
    $c += "$env:ProgramFiles\draw.io\draw.io.exe", "$env:LOCALAPPDATA\Programs\draw.io\draw.io.exe"
    $c += Get-ItemProperty 'HKCU:\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall\*',
                           'HKLM:\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall\*',
                           'HKLM:\Software\WOW6432Node\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall\*' -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
          Where-Object { $_.DisplayName -like '*draw.io*' } |
          ForEach-Object { if ($_.InstallLocation) { Join-Path $_.InstallLocation 'draw.io.exe' } else { ($_.DisplayIcon -split ',')[0] } }
    $c += (Get-PSDrive -PSProvider FileSystem).Root | ForEach-Object { Join-Path $_ 'Program Files\draw.io\draw.io.exe' }
    $c | Where-Object { $_ -and (Test-Path $_) } | Select-Object -First 1
    ```

    $ Get-Content claude-extracted.txt -Encoding UTF8 | Select-Object -Skip 330 -First 220
    Reassignment is a controlled workflow, not a casual drag-and-drop:
    Manager selects a task and clicks “Reassign Task”.
    The system displays the Team Bandwidth Radar, showing current task loads of all team members.
    Manager selects a standardised reassignment reason: Employee Capacity/Overload, Medical/Approved Leave, Skillset Required, or Client Vertical Shift.
    Manager writes a mandatory handover note detailing what was completed and what remains.
    Indelible audit log entry records the transfer. The previous employee is relieved of deadline liability, and the new employee receives an urgent handover alert.
    3.6 The Quality Review & Correction Cycle
    Managerial review is never a blunt “Approve” or “Reject”. Rejection initiates a collaborative correction cycle:
    Employee submits deliverables (files + notes). Task moves to “Waiting for Review”.
    Manager reviews against quality criteria.
    If approved: Task marked “Completed”. Performance points awarded.
    If changes required: Manager returns the submission with itemised deficiency notes. Task re-enters “In Progress (Revision 2)”.
    Employee implements corrections and resubmits with a revision changelog.
    Manager conducts final QA approval. Task formally closed.
    3.7 The Immutable Audit History Component
    Every critical object in IOTS (Tasks, User Accounts, Geofences, Leave Requests, Royalty Settlements) contains an embedded Audit History Ledger.
    Immutable Ledger Rules:
    Append-Only: Audit records can never be updated, overwritten, or deleted by any user, including Super Admins.
    Deterministic Attribution: Every entry records Actor ID, Actor Name, Active Role, Timestamp (ISO-8601 to the millisecond), Origin IP, and Geolocation Context.
    Before & After Delta: Records precisely what changed (e.g., Priority: Normal → Critical).
    Source Distinction: Differentiates between User-Initiated Actions and System-Automated Actions.
    Each audit entry displays: Actor, Role, Timestamp, Action, Object/Resource, Previous Value, New Value, Reason/Comment, and Source (User Action / System Action). The component is strictly read-only and supports filtering by date, actor, action type, and resource.
    
    4. Attendance, Geolocation Engine & Security Auditing
    4.1 Real-Time Availability States
    At any point during the workday, an employee’s live availability status is visible across the system:
    Status
    Indicator
    Definition & Impact
    Available
    Green Solid
    Logged in, at desk or approved workstation, ready to accept new assignments.
    Busy
    Red Solid
    Actively executing a high-priority task. Avoid urgent interruptions.
    In Meeting
    Blue Solid
    Participating in a team standup, zone-wise vertical call, or client briefing.
    On Break
    Amber Solid
    Stepped away for lunch, tea, or personal rest. Work-timer paused.
    In Training
    Indigo Solid
    Undergoing onboarding, compliance modules, or software training.
    Do Not Disturb
    Purple Solid
    Deep focus mode (e.g., Designer formatting an annual report, BDE on focused cold-calling).
    Working Remotely
    Cyan Solid
    Logged in from home or approved off-site location with verified geolocation context.
    On Leave
    Gray Solid
    Formally approved leave. Excluded from daily active task allocations.
    Absent
    Crimson Solid
    Unexcused failure to log in within grace period. Triggers HR attendance flag.
    Offline
    Slate Solid
    Logged off at end of shift.
    4.2 The Geolocation Engine & Office Perimeter Geofencing
    When an employee enters their credentials on the unified login screen, the system executes a three-step security handshake:
    Credential Validation: Verifies Employee ID and Password against the internal directory.
    Hardware GPS Query: Requests the browser’s Hardware GPS API for latitude and longitude coordinates.
    Reverse Geocoding & Perimeter Check: Resolves the coordinates to a human-readable address (via BigDataCloud API), then compares the distance against the assigned office’s approved geofence perimeter using the Haversine formula.
    
    If the employee is within the approved office radius (e.g., 100 metres), the session is tagged “Office / On-Premise” with a green badge. If outside the office perimeter, the session is tagged “Remote / Off-Site” with a calm amber pill and an optional reason memo—the system does NOT block them or treat them as a criminal.
    4.3 Handling Remote & Unknown Locations: Dignity Over Fear
    The IOTS philosophy:
    No frightening error screens. If an employee is outside the office perimeter, they are not blocked.
    Contextual Tagging: A calm message states “We notice you are signing in from outside your designated office perimeter. Your session has been marked as Remote / Off-Site.”
    Optional Reason Memo: The employee can enter a quick note (e.g., “Working from home with prior approval”).
    Manager Notification: The Lead Manager sees a subtle orange pin next to the employee’s name on their live team radar.
    4.4 Attendance & Work Hours Computation
    IOTS eliminates paper registers and manual time tracking:
    Check-In Timestamp: Captured automatically upon first successful login of the day.
    Check-Out Timestamp: Captured when the user clicks “Check-Out & Logoff”.
    Net Work Hours: Active productive time = (Check-Out – Check-In) – Total Break Durations.
    Standard Shift: 9:30 AM to 6:30 PM (9.0 Gross Hours / 8.0 Net Productive Hours).
    Grace Period: 15 minutes. Arrivals up to 9:45 AM marked on-time.
    Late Arrivals: Logins after 9:45 AM flagged. Three unexcused late arrivals per month trigger automated HR and Lead Manager notification.
    4.5 Employee Self-Service Request Portal
    Employees manage their work-life schedules through five request categories:
    Leave Requests: Casual Leave (CL), Sick Leave (SL), Earned/Privilege Leave (PL), and Compensatory Off.
    Short Permission Requests: 1 to 2 hours for doctor appointments, personal emergencies, or government office visits.
    Scheduled Remote Work Requests: Prior approval to work from home for specific days.
    Task Clarification / Guidance Requests: Urgent 15-minute sync with the Lead Manager.
    Operational Resource Requests: Hardware, software licences, commercial portal credits, or conference stationery.
    4.6 Security Auditing & Chairman Anomaly Cockpit
    The Chairman, Super Admin, and IT System Analyst access the Master Security & Location Audit Centre. Automated anomaly detection covers:
    Impossible Travel Velocity: Login in Hyderabad, then same account logs in from Dubai 45 minutes later. System suspends the second session and dispatches urgent alerts.
    Ghost Logins & Session Hijacking: Active sessions are bound to cryptographically signed browser tokens. Copied cookies on a different browser are immediately invalidated.
    Unapproved Remote Clusters: More than 5 consecutive off-site days without approved leave triggers an administrative flag.
    Concurrent Multi-Device Logins: Simultaneous sessions from different IP subnets raise a security investigation event.
    Every login event is preserved with its reverse-geocoded address, GPS coordinates, public IP address, ISP provider, and browser user-agent.
    
    5. CIRIL Commercial Operations & Revenue Architecture
    5.1 The CIRIL Syndication Model
    CIRIL operates as an apex clearinghouse and national alliance of certified commercial real estate advisory firms (Member Companies). The platform provides national umbrella coverage, standardised institutional proposal templates, trusted dispute resolution, proprietary market intelligence, and automated fee distribution.
    5.2 The 4-Tier Geographic Hierarchy
    Country → Zones (North, South, East, West, Central) → States (Regulatory Jurisdictions) → Cities (Metropolitan Commercial Centres) → Member Companies → Advisory Associates.
    5.3 The 6-Phase End-to-End Transaction Lifecycle
    Mandate Origination (Lead Giver Stage): A Member Company in City A secures a mandate from an enterprise client. The broker logs the mandate with comprehensive parameters—space category, vertical type, required area, target budget, and technical specifications.
    Central Clearance & Destination Handoff: CIRIL HQ verifies the mandate and routes it to an accredited Member Company in the destination city (Lead Receiver). The recipient must formally Accept or Reject within 24 hours.
    Sourcing & Institutional Proposals: The Lead Receiver sources inventory and generates standardised presentations—a First-Cut Proposal (comparison matrix) and a Detailed Institutional Proposal (exhaustive building report).
    Site Inspections & 7-Stage Negotiation: The deal advances through Basic Prospecting → Site Inspection → Shortlisting → Commercial Offer → Term Sheet → Legal Drafting → Agreement Execution.
    Deal Conclusion & Financial Lock: Upon execution of the registered lease deed or sale deed, confirmed transaction metrics are locked.
    Fee Apportionment & Royalty Settlement: The system calculates gross brokerage, applies the member split, extracts CIRIL’s 7.5% corporate royalty, and dispatches official conclusion statements.
    5.4 Fee Structure & 7.5% Royalty Engine
    Base Brokerage Calculation:
    Commercial Lease: Gross Brokerage = 1 Month (30 Days) Rental Value.
    Outright Sale / Acquisition: Gross Brokerage = 1.00% of Total Transaction Consideration.
    
    Inter-Member Split:
    Lead Giver (LG) Share: 30% of Gross Brokerage (for origination and client relationship management).
    Lead Receiver (LR) Share: 70% of Gross Brokerage (for local sourcing, inspections, negotiations, and closing).
    
    CIRIL Corporate Royalty = 7.5% of Total Gross Brokerage, deducted proportionally from both LG (7.5% of LG share) and LR (7.5% of LR share).
    
    5.4.1 Financial Simulation: Grade-A Office Lease
    Financial Metric
    Value
    Deal Parameters
    50,000 sq ft @ ₹80 / sq ft / month
    Gross Transaction Value
    ₹40,00,000 / month rent
    Total Gross Brokerage Pool
    ₹40,00,000 (1 Month Rental)
    Lead Giver Share (30%)
    ₹12,00,000
    Lead Receiver Share (70%)
    ₹28,00,000
    Royalty by LG (7.5%)
    ₹90,000
    Royalty by LR (7.5%)
    ₹2,10,000
    Net CIRIL Corporate Royalty
    ₹3,00,000
    Net Retained by LG
    ₹11,10,000
    Net Retained by LR
    ₹25,90,000
    5.5 Proprietary Market Intelligence Engine
    Rental Trends Benchmark: Tracks micro-market rental movements, vacancy absorptions, and effective lease rates.
    Landmark Deals Archive: Catalogs major multi-thousand sq ft leases across BFSI, IT/ITeS, and manufacturing sectors.
    Upcoming Supply Pipeline: Verified registry of under-construction commercial towers and industrial parks.
    Landlord & Tenant Data Mining: Tracks upcoming enterprise lease expirations for early client renewal and mandate origination.
    
    6. Intelligence, Analytics & Automated Reporting
    6.1 The 3 Reporting Rhythms
    Rhythm
    Cadence
    Focus
    Audience
    Daily
    Generated 18:30 IST
    Tasks completed today, overdue items, unresolved blockers, tomorrow’s priorities
    Employees & Lead Managers
    Weekly
    Monday 09:00 IST
    SLA on-time delivery rate, workload rebalancing, team capacity trends
    Senior Managers & General Manager
    Monthly
    1st of Month
    Departmental target fulfilment, royalty settlements, reward system standings
    Chairman & Board of Directors
    6.2 The 6 Analytics Centres
    Centre 1: Performance & Velocity Analytics
    Answers: How fast, how well, and how consistently are teams delivering work?
    Task Completion Rate: Completed Tasks / Total Assigned Tasks × 100.
    On-Time SLA Delivery Rate: Tasks Completed On or Before Deadline / Total Completed Tasks × 100.
    Average Turnaround Duration: Mean time from Assigned to Completed across task categories.
    Rework & Revision Index: Frequency of tasks returned with Changes Requested.
    Centre 2: Task & Workload Distribution Analytics
    Answers: Is work distributed fairly, or are specific individuals becoming bottlenecks?
    Team Workload Capacity Radar: Active task commitments vs. standard 8-hour daily capacity.
    Concentration Analysis: Identifies if 80% of critical tasks are loaded onto 20% of staff.
    Task Pipeline Distribution: Real-time breakdown of tasks in each state.
    Centre 3: Attendance & Workforce Health Analytics
    Punctuality Index, attendance consistency, workplace ratio (On-Premise vs. Remote), and availability patterns.
    Centre 4: Trend & Historical Pattern Analysis
    Time-horizon comparative analysis with toggles: Today vs. Yesterday, This Week vs. Last Week, Last 30 Days vs. Prior 30 Days, and Quarter-to-Date vs. Prior Quarter.
    Centre 5: Exception & Risk Analysis (3-Tier Engine)
    Risk Tier
    Criteria
    System Response
    Tier 1: Low
    Task due in <4h with <30% progress; first-time punctuality flag; single revision requested
    Informational alert in daily digest
    Tier 2: Medium
    Task overdue by 4–24 hours; task reassigned 2+ times; employee capacity exceeds 110%; blocker open >8 hours
    Amber flash in Lead Manager Action Centre; demands active resolution
    Tier 3: High / Critical
    Task overdue >48 hours; unresolved blocker halting commercial deal; repeated unexcused absences; impossible travel anomaly
    Escalates to Senior Manager and Chairman; requires logged intervention
    Centre 6: Predictive Operational Signals
    Deterministic operational warnings based on mathematical thresholds (not fabricated certainty):
    SLA Breach Warning: If 2 hours remain but average completion duration for the task type is 4 hours and progress is only 15%.
    Burnout Early-Warning: If an employee logs >10 net work hours for 4 consecutive days while maintaining >100% capacity.
    Vendor Bottleneck Warning: Historical vendor delivery delays automatically prompting milestone schedule adjustments.
    6.3 Role-Scoped Automated Report Delivery
    To the Chairman: 2-page Executive Radar—enterprise operational health, royalty totals, critical escalations, and 3-month award candidates.
    To General Manager & Senior Managers: Cross-departmental workload matrices, SLA trends, overdue resolution audits.
    To Lead Managers: Actionable daily checklists—tasks needing QA review, pending leave approvals, overdue interventions.
    To Execution Staff: Personal monthly scorecards—completed tasks, on-time delivery %, time tracked, and reward milestone progress.
    
    7. Employee Performance, Reward & Recognition System
    7.1 The Philosophy of Objective Recognition
    In conventional organisations, bonuses and awards are decided through subjective impressions, recency bias, or office politics. This damages morale. The CIRIL IOTS Reward System establishes complete objectivity and transparency. Because every task, delivery timestamp, review cycle, and attendance record is captured accurately, recognition is calculated through transparent mathematical scoring. Every employee can see their live performance score and reward trajectory daily.
    7.2 The 100-Point Performance Scoring Formula
    At the end of each calendar month, the IOTS Intelligence Engine computes an Overall Monthly Performance Score on a 100-point scale:
    
    Component
    Max Points
    Measurement
    Task Delivery & Velocity
    35
    35 × (Completed Tasks / Total Assigned Tasks)
    On-Time SLA Achievement
    30
    30 × (Tasks Completed On or Before Deadline / Total Completed Tasks)

    const fs=require('fs'),z=require('zlib');
    const s=fs.readFileSync('ciril_crm_detail.pdf').toString('latin1');
    const objs=new Map([...s.matchAll(/(\d+) 0 obj\s*([\s\S]*?)endobj/g)].map(m=>[m[1],m[2]]));
    function stream(b){const q=b.match(/stream\r?\n([\s\S]*?)\r?\n?endstream/);return z.inflateSync(Buffer.from(q[1],'latin1')).toString();}
    const maps={};
    for(const [id,b] of objs){const u=b.match(/\/ToUnicode (\d+) 0 R/);if(!u)continue;const c=stream(objs.get(u[1])),map={};
     for(const block of c.matchAll(/beginbfchar([\s\S]*?)endbfchar/g))for(const m of block[1].matchAll(/<([A-Fa-f0-9]+)>\s*<([A-Fa-f0-9]+)>/g))map[parseInt(m[1],16)]=String.fromCodePoint(parseInt(m[2],16));
     for(const block of c.matchAll(/beginbfrange([\s\S]*?)endbfrange/g))for(const m of block[1].matchAll(/<([A-Fa-f0-9]+)>\s*<([A-Fa-f0-9]+)>\s*<([A-Fa-f0-9]+)>/g))for(let i=parseInt(m[1],16);i<=parseInt(m[2],16);i++)map[i]=String.fromCodePoint(parseInt(m[3],16)+i-parseInt(m[1],16));maps[id]=map;
    }
    let output=[],p=0;
    for(const [id,b] of objs){if(!/\/Type \/Page\b/.test(b))continue;const content=stream(objs.get(b.match(/\/Contents (\d+) 0 R/)[1]));let lines=[],lastY=null,line='';
    for(const t of content.matchAll(/BT([\s\S]*?)ET/g)){const f=t[1].match(/\/F(\d+)\s+[\d.]+ Tf/),tm=t[1].match(/([\d.-]+) ([\d.-]+) Tm/),td=t[1].match(/([\d.-]+) ([\d.-]+) Td/);const y=(tm?+tm[2]:0)-(td?+td[2]:0);if(lastY!==null&&Math.abs(y-lastY)>2){lines.push(line);line='';}lastY=y;for(const h of t[1].matchAll(/<([0-9A-Fa-f]+)>/g))for(let i=0;i<h[1].length;i+=4)line+=maps[f?.[1]]?.[parseInt(h[1].slice(i,i+4),16)]||'�';}if(line)lines.push(line);output.push('=== PDF PAGE '+(++p)+' ===\n'+lines.join('\n'));}
    fs.writeFileSync('company-extracted.txt',output.join('\n\n'));
    console.log('Extracted '+p+' pages.');

    CIRIL CRM — Complete System Documentation    |    Page 1
    
    CIRIL CRM
    Complete System Architecture, Business Operations & Revenue Intelligence Manual
    
    For AI Models, Developers & System Architects
    Version 1.0  —  October 2026        Confidential — For Internal and AI Reference Use Only — CIRIL Network © 2026
    
    === PDF PAGE 2 ===
    CIRIL CRM — Complete System Documentation    |    Page 2
       Confidential — For Internal and AI Reference Use Only — CIRIL Network © 2026
    
    === PDF PAGE 3 ===
    CIRIL CRM — Complete System Documentation    |    Page 3
    Section 1 — What is CIRIL?
    CIRIL is a structured, collaborative commercial real estate brokerage network operating across
    India. It is not a single brokerage firm in the traditional sense. Instead, CIRIL functions as a
    franchise umbrella and transaction clearing-house that coordinates multiple independent member
    companies — each a separate real estate brokerage — under one networked platform.
     The word 'CRM' in CIRIL CRM stands for the custom-built software platform that manages the
    complete lifecycle of every real estate transaction that occurs within this network. The software is
    the operational backbone of the business.
    
    1.1 The Core Business Idea
    Most commercial real estate transactions fail or are delayed because the broker who has the client
    (buyer or tenant) is geographically or informationally separate from the broker who has the right
    property. CIRIL solves this problem by creating a shared ecosystem:
     •�� A broker in Pune who has a client looking for a warehouse in Chennai is connected to a
    CIRIL partner broker in Chennai who has inventory.
     •�� The two brokers co-operate. The deal closes. The commission is split. CIRIL takes a royalty
    from both parties.
     •�� The entire process — from lead entry to final payment — is managed inside the CRM.
    1.2 Who are the Actors in the System?
    
    Actor / Role Responsibility Super Admin (roleId = 1) CIRIL's own master administrator. Has visibility over all leads,
    all member companies, all brokerage and royalty data network-wide.
    Zone Manager (roleId = 2) Oversees a specific geographic zone. Can see all leads within
    their zone.
    Member Company Admin Head of a CIRIL partner company. Creates users within their
    org, manages their own leads and network assignments.
    Broker / Agent (User) Day-to-day operator. Creates leads, assigns leads, negotiates
    deals, creates proposals.
    Research Team Feeds market intelligence data (rental trends, major deals, land
    prices) into the Research module.
    1.3 Geographic Hierarchy
    The system enforces a strict, multi-level geographic hierarchy that governs how leads are scoped and
    routed. Every lead is tagged to a location in this hierarchy:
     •�� Zone — The broadest level (e.g., South India, West India). •�� State — Contained within a Zone. •�� City — Contained within a State. This hierarchy means a Zonal Manager can filter the lead pipeline for his entire zone, while a
    member company is typically scoped to one or more cities.
      Confidential — For Internal and AI Reference Use Only — CIRIL Network © 2026
    
    === PDF PAGE 4 ===
    CIRIL CRM — Complete System Documentation    |    Page 4
    Section 2 — What is a Lead? The Core Data Entity
    In CIRIL, a Lead is the most important entity in the entire system. It is the formal record of a client's
    real estate requirement. A lead is NOT a property listing — it is the CLIENT who needs the property.
    
    2.1 Anatomy of a Lead
    A Lead in CIRIL is a composite entity assembled from FOUR separate database tables that are
    always joined together when querying:
    
    Database Table Data It Contains leads The core record. Stores status flags, assignment metadata (user_id,
    assigned_to, assigned_orgid, assigned_user_id), negotiation document references (LOI file, Lease file), and all timestamps.
    client_details Who is the client? Stores client name, company name, contact info,
    document type (Aadhar, PAN, etc.), client type (individual or company).
    requirements_details What does the client need? Stores the vertical, transaction type, area range,
    budget, city, zone, and all vertical-specific technical parameters.
    financial_details How is the commission structured? Stores giver share %, receiver share %,
    giver and receiver names, their contact details, and brokerage status flags.
    2.2 Lead Statuses — The State Machine
    Every lead moves through a numbered status pipeline. Understanding these statuses is critical to
    understanding how revenue is recognized:
    
    Statu
    s ID
    Status Name Meaning 1 New / Open Lead just created. No action taken. 2 Processing Lead is being worked on by a broker. 3 Assigned Lead has been shared/assigned to another member company. 4 Negotiation Active deal negotiations are ongoing. Broker uploads LOI, Lease
    Agreement.
    5 Rejected Lead was rejected by the receiving broker. 6 Deal Done The deal is closed. Brokerage calculation is triggered. 7 Brokerage Accepted Both parties (giver and receiver) have accepted the calculated
    brokerage split.
    8 Royalty Paid CIRIL's royalty has been confirmed as paid. FINAL state.
      Confidential — For Internal and AI Reference Use Only — CIRIL Network © 2026
    
    === PDF PAGE 5 ===
    CIRIL CRM — Complete System Documentation    |    Page 5
    Section 3 — The Lead Giver & Lead Receiver: The
    Heart of CIRIL
    The most important conceptual distinction in the entire CIRIL model is the difference between the
    Lead Giver and the Lead Receiver. Every financial calculation in the system flows from this
    relationship.
    
    3.1 The Lead Giver (LG)
    LEAD GIVER
    The broker or member company who originated the lead — i.e., the person
    who found the client and brought them into the CIRIL network. The Lead Giver owns the client relationship.
    In the database, the Lead Giver is identified by the leads.user_id field — the user who created the
    lead record. Their financial terms are stored in financial_details under financial_giver_share (their
    percentage of the total brokerage) and fields like financial_giver_name, financial_giver_phoneno.
     Scenario: Rajesh, a broker at CIRIL Mumbai, gets approached by TCS who wants 50,000 sq. ft. of
    office space in Bengaluru. Rajesh does not have Bengaluru inventory. He creates the lead in CIRIL
    CRM. Rajesh is now the Lead Giver.
    
    3.2 The Lead Receiver (LR)
    LEAD RECEIVER
    The broker or member company who receives the assigned lead from the giver
    and takes responsibility for finding and closing the property transaction in their territory.
    In the database, the Lead Receiver is identified by the leads.assigned_to field (company) and
    leads.assigned_user_id (individual broker). Their financial terms are stored in financial_details
    under financial_receiver_share (their percentage of the total brokerage).
     Scenario: Priya, a broker at CIRIL Bengaluru, receives Rajesh's TCS lead. She searches her
    inventory, presents properties, manages site visits, and negotiates the lease. Priya is the Lead
    Receiver.
    
    3.3 The Assignment Workflow in Code
    When a lead is assigned via the AssignleadController, the system updates three critical fields: •�� leads.assigned_to → set to the receiver's member company name/ID •�� leads.assigned_orgid → set to the receiver's organization ID (orgId) •�� leads.is_assigned → set to 1 (true) The receiver then sees this lead in their dashboard under 'Assign Lead'. They must explicitly click
    Accept Lead (acceptLead method) to confirm their participation, which updates leads.accepted = 1
    and records leads.accepted_datetime.
     •�� If the receiver rejects the lead, they call storeRejectLeadRemarks, which stores rejection
    remarks and resets the lead.
      Confidential — For Internal and AI Reference Use Only — CIRIL Network © 2026
    
    === PDF PAGE 6 ===
    CIRIL CRM — Complete System Documentation    |    Page 6
    Section 4 — Revenue Engine: How CIRIL Makes
    Money
    CIRIL generates its revenue exclusively through Royalties — a fixed percentage cut taken from both
    the Lead Giver's share and the Lead Receiver's share of every successfully concluded deal's
    brokerage fee. The actual brokerage is split between LG and LR. CIRIL takes 7.5% from each party's
    share.
     The entire calculation is hard-coded in BrokerageController.php (getBrokerage method, line 202)
    and is fully automated — the system computes it the moment a deal reaches negotiation status.
    
    4.1 Step 1 — Calculate the Base Deal Value (Transaction Value)
    The base value depends on whether the transaction is a Lease (monthly rent) or a Sale (outright
    purchase):
      LEASE
    Total Value = Rent Rate per sq. ft. per month × Area (sq. ft.)
     SALE
    Total Value = Outright Rate per sq. ft. × Area (sq. ft.)
    4.2 Step 2 — Calculate Total Brokerage
    The brokerage due from the client is calculated differently for lease vs. sale:
    LEASE: Total Brokerage = Total Value × (Lease Days / 30)
    
    SALE:  Total Brokerage = 1% × Total Value
    
    The 'Lease Days' is the agreed number of days of rent that constitute the brokerage fee (a
    commercial real estate convention in India — typically 30-90 days of rent).
    
    4.3 Step 3 — Split the Brokerage Between LG and LR
    The brokerage split is agreed upon upfront when the lead is created and stored in the
    FinancialDetail record. The fields financial_giver_share and financial_receiver_share contain the
    agreed percentage for each party. If either is set to 'others', the system reads
    financial_giver_share_others or financial_receiver_share_others for a custom percentage.
    
    LG Share (Rs.) = (financial_giver_share / 100) × Total
    Brokerage
    LR Share (Rs.) = (financial_receiver_share / 100) × Total
    Brokerage
    4.4 Step 4 — CIRIL Royalty Calculation (The Revenue Formula)
    Confidential — For Internal and AI Reference Use Only — CIRIL Network © 2026
    
    === PDF PAGE 7 ===
    CIRIL CRM — Complete System Documentation    |    Page 7
    This is CIRIL's revenue. CIRIL charges 7.5% of the LG's share AND 7.5% of the LR's share as a
    royalty. This is the hard-coded business rule extracted directly from BrokerageController.php line
    202:
    
    CIRIL Royalty = ROUND( (7.5/100 × LG Share) + (7.5/100 × LR
    Share) )
    This is effectively 7.5% of the total brokerage (since LG% + LR% = 100%), but it is calculated
    per-party so that each broker understands exactly how much they owe CIRIL.
    
    4.5 Full Worked Example — A Lease Deal
    Scenario: TCS signs a lease for 50,000 sq. ft. of office space in Bengaluru. Details:  Transaction Type : Lease
    Area             : 50,000 sq. ft. Rent Rate        : Rs. 80 per sq. ft. per month Lease Days       : 30 days (1 month's rent as brokerage) LG Share         : 40%  (Rajesh, Mumbai) LR Share         : 60%  (Priya, Bengaluru) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────── >> Step 1:  Total Value      = 80 × 50,000            = Rs. 40,00,000 >> Step 2:  Total Brokerage  = 40,00,000 × (30/30)    = Rs. 40,00,000 >> Step 3:  LG Share (40%)   = 0.40 × 40,00,000       = Rs. 16,00,000            LR Share (60%)   = 0.60 × 40,00,000       = Rs. 24,00,000 >> Step 4:  CIRIL Royalty    = (7.5% × 16,00,000) + (7.5% × 24,00,000)                             = Rs. 1,20,000 + Rs. 1,80,000            CIRIL Royalty    = Rs. 3,00,000 ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────── >> Rajesh Net               = Rs. 16,00,000 - Rs. 1,20,000  = Rs. 14,80,000 >> Priya Net                = Rs. 24,00,000 - Rs. 1,80,000  = Rs. 22,20,000 >> CIRIL Revenue            = Rs. 3,00,000 (7.5% from each party)
    4.6 Full Worked Example — A Sale/Outright Deal
    Scenario: A land parcel of 2 acres sold for Rs. 1,200 per sq. ft. (approx. 87,120 sq. ft.) at a standard
    1% brokerage. LG/LR split is 50/50.
      Transaction Type : Sale (Outright)
    Area             : 87,120 sq. ft.  (2 acres) Outright Rate    : Rs. 1,200 per sq. ft. LG Share         : 50% LR Share         : 50% ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────── >> Step 1:  Total Value      = 1,200 × 87,120          = Rs. 10,45,44,000
    Confidential — For Internal and AI Reference Use Only — CIRIL Network © 2026
    
    === PDF PAGE 8 ===
    CIRIL CRM — Complete System Documentation    |    Page 8 >> Step 2:  Total Brokerage  = 1% × 10,45,44,000       = Rs. 10,45,440 >> Step 3:  LG Share (50%)   = 0.50 × 10,45,440        = Rs. 5,22,720            LR Share (50%)   = 0.50 × 10,45,440        = Rs. 5,22,720 >> Step 4:  CIRIL Royalty    = (7.5% × 5,22,720) + (7.5% × 5,22,720)                             = Rs. 39,204 + Rs. 39,204            CIRIL Royalty    = Rs. 78,408 ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────── >> LG Net                   = Rs. 5,22,720 - Rs. 39,204  = Rs. 4,83,516 >> LR Net                   = Rs. 5,22,720 - Rs. 39,204  = Rs. 4,83,516 >> CIRIL Revenue            = Rs. 78,408  Confidential — For Internal and AI Reference Use Only — CIRIL Network © 2026
