    Section 5 — The Complete Deal Lifecycle (End to
    End)
    Every rupee of CIRIL's revenue follows this exact sequence. The CRM enforces this pipeline through
    status codes and controller logic.
    
    Phase 1 — Lead Creation (LeadController@createLead)
    The broker (Lead Giver) opens the 'Create Lead' form. This is the most data-intensive step in the
    system. The form collects data that populates ALL FOUR lead tables simultaneously:
     •�� Client Details: Company name, person name, mobile, email, client type
    (individual/company), document type and file upload.
     •�� Requirements Details: Vertical selection (Office / Retail / Land / Warehouse / Investment /
    Hotels) → triggers a dynamic sub-form with vertical-specific fields.
     •�� Financial Details: The giver and receiver's expected shares, giver's brokerage terms — filled
    at creation based on negotiated terms.
     This design means a lead is never a partial record — all four tables must have data for a lead to be
    valid.
    
    Phase 2 — Vertical-Specific Data Entry
    Once a vertical is selected, the requirements form dynamically loads unique fields for that property
    type. This is managed by VerticalType and VerticalSubType models and the CustomFieldsController.
    Key examples:
    
    Vertical Unique Required Fields Office Space Carpet area, office type (pre-leased/vacant), completion date, power backup,
    car parking count.
    Retail Height from floor (from/to), floor level (from/to), retail status, completion
    date.
    Logistic & Warehouse
    (LW)
    Height from (ft), height to (ft), floor type, open area (from/to), covered area
    (from/to), power type (single/three phase), roofing type, LW status.
    Land Frontage width (from/to), road width (from/to), power availability, power
    type, land status (agricultural/non-agricultural).
    Investment Constructed area (from/to), lead area (from/to), investment type
    (pre-launch/ready), pre-property details, investment location.
    Hotels Number of keys (from/to), agreement type (management/lease/sale).
    Phase 3 — Lead Assignment (AssignleadController)
    Once the LG cannot fulfill the requirement alone, they assign the lead through the network. The
    system:
     Searches for member companies in the target city using the geographic hierarchy (Zone → State →
    City).
     Updates leads.assigned_to, leads.assigned_orgid, leads.is_assigned = 1. Fires a SendNotificationsEvent which creates a real-time notification for the receiver. Confidential — For Internal and AI Reference Use Only — CIRIL Network © 2026
    
    === PDF PAGE 10 ===
    ✓ • 1.21s

## Activity

    $ Get-Content company-extracted.txt -Encoding UTF8 | Select-Object -Skip 230; Get-Content Construct.txt -Encoding UTF8 | Select-Object -First 200
    CIRIL CRM — Complete System Documentation    |    Page 10
    The LR sees the lead on their 'Assign Lead' dashboard with Accept / Reject options. On accept: leads.accepted = 1 is written. The deal is now active for both parties.
    Phase 4 — Negotiation (LeadController@negotiation)
    The LR is now in active negotiation with the client. They update the negotiation_status_id field with
    progress codes. Most critically, when a deal is nearly closed, they upload two legal documents
    directly via the CRM:
     •�� LOI (Letter of Intent) — stored in leads.negotiation_loi_file •�� Final Lease/Sale Agreement — stored in leads.negotiation_lease_file The system tracks negotiation status through IDs 1-7. Status ID 7 means 'Deal Concluded' and
    triggers the move to brokerage.
    
    Phase 5 — Proposal Generation
    (BrokerageController@addproposel / adddetailedproposel)
    Before or during negotiation, brokers can generate formal client proposals. The system supports two
    types:
     •�� First-Cut Proposal (type='F'): A quick preliminary proposal. For Land vertical, generates a
    PowerPoint (.pptx) file using PHPPresentation library. For all others, generates an Excel
    (.xls) file using PhpSpreadsheet. Both include the CIRIL logo.
     •�� Detailed Proposal (type='D'): Full property analysis with image uploads for land sites. Same
    file format logic applies.
     Proposal files are saved to the /proposels/ directory and referenced in the proposels table with
    lead_id and type fields.
    
    Phase 6 — Brokerage Calculation
    (BrokerageController@getBrokerage)
    When leads.lead_status reaches 4 (Negotiation/Deal Done) AND leads.negotiation_status_id
    reaches 7, the BrokerageController automatically calculates the full financial breakdown and writes
    it to the brokerage table. The brokerage record contains:
     •�� lead_id, clientType (lease/sale), area, rate (rentRate or outrightRate) •�� total (base deal value), totalBrokerage, LGshare (Rs.), LRshare (Rs.) •�� cirilRoyalty (Rs.) — CIRIL's revenue from this deal •�� status — empty until accepted, then 'accepted' •�� royality — 0 until royalty payment confirmed, then 1
    Phase 7 — Brokerage Acceptance
    (BrokerageController@brokerageStatus)
    The LR (the receiver who will collect and distribute the brokerage) formally accepts the calculated
    split. This sets leads.lead_status = 7 ('Brokerage Accepted'). At this point both parties are legally
    committed to the calculated split, and CIRIL's royalty obligation is triggered.
    
    Phase 8 — Royalty Confirmation
    (RoyaltyController@royalityStatus)
    This is the final financial step and CIRIL's revenue recognition event. When the Super Admin
    confirms that royalty has been paid:
     Confidential — For Internal and AI Reference Use Only — CIRIL Network © 2026

    CIRIL CRM — Complete System Documentation    |    Page 11
    leads.lead_status is updated to 8 ('Royalty Paid'). leads.leadStatus = 3 (closed). A new entry is inserted into lead_statuses confirming the vertical and organization. brokerage.royality is set to 1, permanently marking this deal as completed. The system sends a 'Lead Concluded' email to the Admin, the Lead Giver, and the Lead Receiver
    (CC'd) with full deal details.
     A real-time notification is fired via SendNotificationsEvent. The Royalty Payment Status endpoint (royalitypaymentstatus) also allows recording individual
    payment dates and remarks in the payments table for financial audit tracking.

    Market intelligence data tables.
    6.4 Authentication & Authorization (RBAC)
    Every route in web.php is protected by two middleware layers: •�� api — Ensures the request goes through the API middleware stack (CSRF, session). •�� auth — Ensures the user is logged in (Laravel Sanctum session check). •�� role — A custom middleware that checks the user's roleId and orgId against the module's
    required permission level. This is what prevents a broker from seeing another company's
    leads.
     The Admin (roleId = 1) is the only user who can see ALL leads across ALL companies network-wide.
    All other roles are scoped to their orgId or assigned leads.
      Confidential — For Internal and AI Reference Use Only — CIRIL Network © 2026

    Section 7 — The Research & Intelligence Engine
    Beyond transaction management, CIRIL operates an internal market intelligence function that gives
    its brokers a competitive edge when pitching to clients. This is managed by the
    ResearchReportController and its associated models.
    
    7.1 Research Verticals Supported
    •�� Commercial / Office — Tracks rental trends, major deals in each city, upcoming commercial
    projects.
     •�� Land — Tracks land pricing trends, major land transactions, township/project
    developments.
     •�� Warehouse & Logistics — Tracks warehouse rental trends, major warehouse leasing deals.
    7.2 Data Models in the Research Engine
    
    Model Data Tracked ResearchReport Master research report entry. Links to a vertical and period. ResearchRentaltrend Quarter-by-quarter rental rate data per micro-market/city. ResearchMajorDeal Records of significant leasing or sale deals with tenant, area,
    building.
    ResearchMajorUpcoming Pipeline of upcoming commercial projects expected to enter the
    market.
    ResearchLandModel Land-specific report entry. ResearchLandtrend Land price trends per location per period. ResearchMajorDeal (Land) Major land purchase transactions. ResearchrentalwarehouseMod
    el
    Warehouse rental trend data. Researchmajordealswarehous
    eModel
    Major warehouse leasing transactions.
    7.3 How Research Drives Revenue
    When a broker creates a Detailed Proposal for a client, the system can pull current rental trends and
    major deal benchmarks from the research module to populate the proposal with real market data.
    This makes CIRIL proposals significantly more credible than generic broker documents, increasing
    deal closure rates — which directly increases CIRIL's royalty income.

    Section 8 — Notifications & Real-Time Event System
    The CIRIL CRM includes a real-time notification system that keeps all parties informed as lead
    statuses change. This system uses Laravel's event broadcasting pattern.
    
    8.1 The SendNotificationsEvent
    Every significant action in the system fires a SendNotificationsEvent. This event carries a payload
    with lead_id, url (the deep link to navigate to the relevant screen), and type (the category of
    notification). Key notification types:
    
    Notification Type When It Fires first-cut-proposal Fired when a First-Cut Proposal is generated and available for
    download.
    royality-update Fired when royalty status is confirmed by the admin. lead-assigned Fired when a lead is assigned to a receiver company. lead-accepted Fired when the receiver formally accepts the lead. lead-rejected Fired when a receiver rejects an assigned lead.
    8.2 Notification Storage
    Notifications are persisted in the notifications table (mapped to the NotificationsModel). The
    NotificationsController provides three actions: mark_as_read (individual), clear_all (bulk), and
    view all (paginated list). This means even if a user is offline when an event fires, they see the
    notification on next login.
      Confidential — For Internal and AI Reference Use Only — CIRIL Network © 2026
    
    === PDF PAGE 17 ===
    CIRIL CRM — Complete System Documentation    |    Page 17
    Section 9 — Reporting & Analytics Engine
    The CIRIL CRM provides a multi-dimensional reporting module that allows management to analyze
    the deal pipeline and network performance. The ReportController and ReportController2 serve
    these reports.
    
    9.1 Report Dimensions
    •�� Time-Filtered Report — View all leads in a date range. Default view for day-to-day
    monitoring.
     •�� City-Wise Report — Aggregate deal volume and value per city. Uses reportCityFilter for
    interactive drilling.
     •�� Vertical-Wise Report — Performance broken down by Office, Retail, Land, Warehouse, etc. •�� Zonal Report — High-level aggregate for zone managers showing performance across their
    entire zone.
    
    9.2 Admin vs. Member Company Reporting
    Role-based scoping applies to reports too: •�� Admin (roleId = 1) sees network-wide aggregates — total deals closed, total royalty earned,
    best-performing cities, top member companies.
     •�� Zone Managers see their zone's performance. •�� Member Companies see only their own leads and their share of brokerage.  Confidential — For Internal and AI Reference Use Only — CIRIL Network © 2026

    Section 10 — Guidelines, Brand Standards &
    Additional Modules
    10.1 Guidelines Module (GuideLinesController)
    CIRIL maintains operational guidelines accessible within the CRM: •�� Stationary Specification — Guidelines for how member companies must use CIRIL brand
    elements (logos, letterheads, business cards) on physical stationery.
     •�� Invoice Generation — Standard invoice template guidelines for billing clients for brokerage. •�� Data Mining Guide — Instructions on how to prospect and mine data for new client leads. •�� Property Listing Guide — Standards for how properties should be listed and described in
    proposals.
    
    10.2 Individual Data Mining (IndividualMiningController)
    Brokers can log individual prospect contacts they are personally mining — people who have not yet
    committed to a formal lead. This acts as a pre-lead pipeline that brokers can convert into full leads
    when the prospect is ready. The model IndividualMining stores name, contact, property interest,
    and status.
    
    10.3 Miscellaneous Approvals (MiscApprovalsController)
    Brokers can request approval to operate in additional 'Verticals' beyond their base assignment. For
    example, a broker primarily doing Office deals may request approval to also handle Warehouse
    leads. These requests are managed through the verticals approval workflow, where the Admin
    approves or denies the expansion of a broker's vertical scope.
    
    10.4 Release Notes Module (ReleasenoteController)
    The CRM includes a formal Release Notes module where the CIRIL tech team publishes changelogs
    for new features and fixes. This is accessible to all users and serves as an in-app changelog for the
    platform itself.
      Confidential — For Internal and AI Reference Use Only — CIRIL Network © 2026

    Section 11 — System Summary for AI Models
    For any AI model, developer, or agent working with this codebase, the following mental model is
    critical:
    
    11.1 The Golden Mental Model
     THINK OF IT AS
    CIRIL CRM is a Commission-Splitting Real Estate Network where every
    transaction generates a 7.5% royalty for CIRIL from each party involved. The software enforces the entire pipeline from client intake to payment confirmation.
    Every feature in the codebase maps to one of these core needs:
    •�� Intake — Lead creation and vertical-specific data capture. •�� Routing — Assignment of leads across the geographic network. •�� Tracking — Negotiation status, document uploads, remarks history. •�� Revenue Calculation — The brokerage engine with the hardcoded 7.5% CIRIL royalty. •�� Revenue Recognition — Royalty confirmation, email notification, final payment logging. •�� Intelligence — Research reports to improve proposal quality and deal closure rates. •�� Governance — RBAC ensuring every user sees only what they are entitled to see.
    11.2 Key Code Locations for AI Reference

    s

    negotiation_status_id

    === PDF PAGE 20 ===
    CIRIL CRM — Complete System Documentation    |    Page 20
    11.3 What CIRIL Is NOT
    •�� It is NOT a property listing portal. There is no 'properties' table. The system manages client
    requirements and deal progress, not property inventory.
     •�� It is NOT a single-company CRM. It is explicitly multi-tenant, with each member company
    having its own isolated data view enforced by RBAC.
     •�� It is NOT an SPA (Single Page Application). Every page is a server-rendered Blade template.
    JavaScript is used for interactivity within pages but routing is server-side.

    IOTS
    │

    │   │

    │   ├── Credential Validation
    │   │   ├── Valid → Continue
    │   │   └── Invalid → Notify Invalid Credentials → Return
    │   │
    │   ├── Location Validation

        public function getbrokerageData($id)
        {
            try {
                $brokerage = DB::table('brokerage')->where('lead_id', $id)->first();
                return $this->SuccessStatus($brokerage);
            } catch (\Exception $e) {
                return $this->errorStatus('json', $e);
            }
        }
        public function createbrokerage(Request $request)
        {
            try {
                $moduleId = $request->moduleId;
                $rule = [
                    'clientType' => ['required'],
                    'area' => ['required', 'numeric'],
                ];
                $message = [
                    'clientType.required' => 'Client type is required',
                    'area.required' => 'Area is required',
                    'area.numeric' => 'Enter digits only',
                ];
                $validator = Validator::make($request->all(), $rule, $message);
                if ($validator->fails()) {
                    return $this->validationStatus($validator->errors());
                }
                if ($request->clientType == 'lease' && empty($request->leaseDays)) {
                    return $this->validationStatus(['lease days required']);
                }
                if ($request->clientType == 'lease' && empty($request->rentRate)) {
                    return $this->validationStatus(['Rent rate days required']);
                }
                if ($request->clientType == 'sale' && empty($request->outrightRate)) {
                    return $this->validationStatus(['Outright rate days required']);
                }
                $requests = $request->all();
                unset($requests['_token']);
                $id = $request->id;
                if (!empty($id)) {
                    $brokerage =  DB::table('brokerage')->where('id', $id)->update($requests);
                } else {
                    $brokerage =   DB::table('brokerage')->insert($requests);
                }
    
                return $this->SuccessStatus($brokerage);
            } catch (\Exception $e) {
                return $this->errorStatus('json', $e);
            }
        }
        public function getBrokerage($id = NULL)
        {
            try {
                $financial_brokerage = FinancialDetail::where('lead_id', $id)->first();
                if ($financial_brokerage->financial_giver_share == 'others') {
                    $giverPercentage = $financial_brokerage->financial_giver_share_others;
                } else {
                    $giverPercentage = $financial_brokerage->financial_giver_share;
                }
                if ($financial_brokerage->financial_receiver_share == 'others') {
                    $receiverPercentage = $financial_brokerage->financial_receiver_share_others;
                } else {
                    $receiverPercentage = $financial_brokerage->financial_receiver_share;
                }
                $brokerageData = DB::table('brokerage')->where('lead_id', $id)->first();
                $financial_brokerage = FinancialDetail::where('lead_id', $id)->first();
                $leads = Lead::find($id);
                $selectRequirement = RequirementsDetail::where('lead_id', $id)->first();
    
                $data = [];
    
                if (empty($brokerageData->status)) {
                    if (!empty($brokerageData)) {
                        $clientType = $brokerageData->clientType;
                        $area = $brokerageData->area;
                        $leaseDays = $brokerageData->leaseDays;
                    } else {
                        $clientType = $selectRequirement->transaction_type;
                        $area = 0;
                        $leaseDays = 0;
                    }
                    if (!empty($brokerageData)) {
                        if ($clientType == 'lease') {
                            $rate = $brokerageData->rentRate;
                        } else {
                            $rate = $brokerageData->outrightRate;
                        }
                    }
    
                    if (empty($rate)) {
                        $rate = 0;
                    }
    
                    $total = $rate * $area;
                    if ($clientType == 'lease') {
                        $totalBrokerage = round($total * ($leaseDays / 30));
                    } else {
                        $totalBrokerage = round((1 / 100) * $total);
                    }
                    $LGshare = round(($giverPercentage / 100) * $totalBrokerage);
                    $LRshare = round(($receiverPercentage / 100) * $totalBrokerage);
                    $cirilRoyalty = round(((7.5 / 100) * $LGshare) + ((7.5 / 100) * $LRshare));
                    $data['lead_id'] = $id;
                    $data['clientType'] = $clientType;
                    $data['area'] = $area;
                    $data['rate'] = $rate;
                    $data['total'] = $total;
                    $data['totalBrokerage'] = $totalBrokerage;
                    $data['LGshare'] = $LGshare;
                    $data['LRshare'] = $LRshare;
                    $data['cirilRoyalty'] = $cirilRoyalty;
                    $data['status'] = '';
    
    
                    if (empty($brokerageData)) {
                        $data['createdBy'] = Auth()->user()->id;
                        DB::table('brokerage')->insert($data);
                        $data['id'] = DB::table('brokerage')->where('lead_id', $id)->value('id');
                    } else {
                        $data['updatedBy'] = Auth()->user()->id;
                        DB::table('brokerage')->where('lead_id', $id)->update($data);
                        $data['id'] = $brokerageData->id;
                    }
                } else {
                    $data = (array) $brokerageData;
                }
    
                return view('Leads/getBrokerage', ['data' => $data]);
            } catch (\Exception $e) {
                return $this->errorStatus('view', $e);
            }
        }
        public function addproposel($id = null)
        {
    
            $req = DB::TABLE('requirements_details')->where('lead_id', $id)->value('selectRequirement');
            $proposels = ProposelsModel::where(['lead_id' => $id, 'type' => 'F'])->get();
            $string = "";
            $images = [];
            if ($req) {
                if ($req == 'retail') {
                    $fields = $this->retail();
                } elseif ($req == 'land') {
                    $fields = $this->firstcutLand();
                    $images = BrokerageController::$firstcutLandImages;
                } elseif ($req == 'office') {
                    $fields = $this->office();
                } elseif ($req == 'Logistic And Warehouse') {
                    $fields = $this->industrial();
                } else {
                    $fields = $this->office();
                }
                $filedsRequiredOffice = config('globalConfig.requiredFiledsOffice');
                $starting = '<div class="col-md-6"><div class="form-group">';
                $endding = '</div></div>';
                if (!empty($fields)) {
                    $i=0;
                    foreach ($fields as $k => $field) {
                        if( $field['required'] == 'required'){
                        $lable = '<label for="' . $k . '">' . $k .'<sup>*</sup></label>';
                        } else {
                            $lable = '<label for="' . $k . '">' . $k .'<sup></sup></label>';
                        }
                        $input = '<input type="' . $field['type'] . '" value="" class="form-control " name="column' . $i . '" id="column' . $i . '" ' . $field['required'] . '>';
                        $string .= $starting . $lable . $input . $endding;
                        $i++;
                    }
                }
            }
            return view('Leads/addBrokerage', ['string' => $string, 'lead_id' => $id, 'proposels' => $proposels]);
        }
        public function adddetailedproposel($id = null)
        {
            $req = DB::TABLE('requirements_details')->where('lead_id', $id)->value('selectRequirement');
            $proposels = ProposelsModel::where(['lead_id' => $id, 'type' => 'D'])->get();
            $string = "";
            $images = [];
            if ($req) {
                if ($req == 'retail') {
                    $fields = $this->retail();
                } elseif ($req == 'land') {
                    $fields = $this->detailedLand();
                    $images = BrokerageController::$detailedLandImages;
                } elseif ($req == 'office') {
                    $fields = $this->office();
                } elseif ($req == 'Logistic And Warehouse') {
                    $fields = $this->industrial();
                } else {
                    $fields = $this->office();
                }
    
    
                $starting = '<div class="col-md-6"><div class="form-group">';
                $endding = '</div></div>';
                if (!empty($fields)) {
                    foreach ($fields as $i => $field) {
                        $inputtype = "text";
                        if (in_array($field, $images)) {
                            $inputtype = "file";
                        }
                        $name = 'column'.$i;
                        $lable = '<label for="' . $i . '">' . $i . '</label>';
                        $input = '<input type="' . $inputtype . '" value="" class="form-control" name="'.$name.'" id="column' . $i . '" >';
                        $string .= $starting . $lable . $input . $endding;
                    }
                }
            }
            return view('Leads/detailedproposel', ['string' => $string, 'lead_id' => $id, 'proposels' => $proposels]);
        }

        --------------------------------------
        Core Manager-Employee Task Lifecycle
        --------------------------------------
    
        MANAGER
           ↓
        Create Task
           ↓
        Define Deliverables
           ↓
        Set Priority & Due Date
           ↓
        Check Employee Workload
           ↓
        Assign Employee
           ↓
        Notify Employee
           ↓
        EMPLOYEE
           ↓
        Start / Continue Work
           ↓
        Update Progress
           ↓
        Record Work Activity
           ↓
        Upload Evidence
           ↓
        Submit Work
           ↓
        MANAGER
           ↓
        Review Task
           ↓
        Approved?
           ├── YES
           │    ↓
           │  Complete & Close
           │
           └── NO
                ↓
             Feedback / Corrections
                ↓
             Employee Revision
                ↓
             Resubmit
                ↓
             Manager Review
    
        ----------------------------------
        Exception / Intervention Paths:
        ----------------------------------
    
        1. Overdue:
    
            Active Task
                ↓
            Deadline Reached
                ↓
            OVERDUE
                ↓
            Manager Reviews
                ↓
            Resolution?
            ├── Extend Deadline
            ├── Reassign Task
            └── Escalate / Intervene
    
        2. Reassignment:
    
            Task
             ↓
            Reassign
             ↓
            Check Employee Workload
             ↓
            Choose New Employee
             ↓
            Notify New Owner
             ↓
            Continue Task
             ↓
            Audit Change
    
        3. Issue / Blocker
    
            Employee Encounters Issue
                    ↓
            Raise Issue Ticket
                    ↓
            Assign Severity
                    ↓
            Manager Intervention
                    ↓
            Resolve?
            ├── YES → Employee Continues Work
            └── NO  → Escalate to Higher Authority
    
        4. Review / Correction
    
            Employee Submission
                    ↓
            Manager Review
                    ↓
            Approved?
            ├── YES → Complete / Close
            └── NO  → Feedback / Corrections
                           ↓
                        Resubmit

        ----------------------------------------------------
            Shared IOTS Services
            │
            ├── Authentication
            ├── Role-Based Access Control
            ├── Location Verification
            ├── Attendance
            ├── Task Workflow Engine
            ├── Notifications

            ├── Issue / Escalation Handling
            ├── Audit Logging
            ├── Reporting
            └── Intelligence & Analytics
    
    -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    INTELLIGENCE & ANALYSIS LAYER
    -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    
        PURPOSE
        -------
        The Intelligence and Analytical Layer converts operational records generated by the
        IOTS into measurable performance indicators, workload insights, attendance patterns,
        exceptions, risks, trends, and management reports.
    
        This layer does not directly perform day-to-day work operations.
        Instead, it analyzes the records created by the Management Layer and presents
        meaningful information to Employees, Managers, and higher-level management according
        to their authorized scope.
    
        The layer should support:
        - Historical analysis
        - Current operational analysis
        - Trend identification
        - Performance measurement
        - Workload analysis
        - Attendance analysis
        - Exception detection
        - Risk classification
        - Management reporting
        - Role-based visibility
        - Drill-down from summary information to underlying records
    
    
        1. PERFORMANCE ANALYTICS
        ========================
    
        FUNCTION
        --------
        Performance Analytics measures how effectively an individual employee, manager,
        team, role, or organizational unit is completing assigned work against defined
        expectations.
    
        The system should not rely on a single performance value. It should combine
        multiple measurable indicators so that performance represents actual work outcomes
        rather than only task volume.
    
    
        1.1 TASK COMPLETION RATE
        ------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures the percentage of assigned tasks that were completed within the selected
        analysis period.
    
        Logic:
    
        Completion Rate =
        (Number of tasks completed / Number of tasks that were due or assigned for the
        selected measurement period) × 100
    
        Handling Logic:
        - Identify all tasks belonging to the selected employee, manager, team, role, or
          organizational scope.
        - Determine which tasks fall inside the selected reporting period.
        - Count tasks whose final workflow outcome is Completed / Closed.
        - Count tasks that were expected to be completed during that period.
        - Exclude cancelled tasks from completion calculations where cancellation is an
          administrative outcome rather than employee non-completion.
        - Reassigned tasks should retain their history and ownership information.
        - Reassignment must not create duplicate task counts.
        - Display the result together with the total number of tasks used in the calculation.
        - Allow the user to drill from the percentage into the underlying task records.
    
        Possible Output:
        - Completion Rate: 91%
        - Completed: 41
        - Expected: 45
        - Remaining: 4
    
    
        1.2 ON-TIME COMPLETION
        ----------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures how many completed tasks were completed on or before their applicable
        deadline.
    
        Logic:
        - Compare the actual completion/approval timestamp against the task deadline.
        - A task completed on or before the deadline is classified as On-Time.
        - A task completed after the deadline is classified as Late.
        - Tasks whose deadlines were officially extended should use the latest authorized
          deadline for the final comparison, while retaining the original deadline in
          historical records.
        - Tasks that became overdue and were later completed remain visible as late
          completions.
        - Display both on-time percentage and late-completion count.
    
        Output:
        - On-Time Completion Rate
        - Late Completion Rate
        - Number of tasks completed on time
        - Number of tasks completed late
        - Average delay duration for late tasks
    
    
        1.3 AVERAGE COMPLETION TIME
        ---------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures the average time required to move tasks from active execution to
        completion.
    
        Logic:
        - Determine the defined start point for the task.
        - Determine the actual completion point.
        - Calculate the elapsed completion duration.
        - Where the workflow records blocked/waiting periods, the reporting logic may
          distinguish total elapsed time from active work time.
        - Use a consistent business definition throughout the organization.
        - Do not mix gross elapsed duration and active work duration without explicitly
          labelling them.
        - Display the average together with task volume because a small sample can distort
          interpretation.
    
        Output:
        - Average completion time
        - Median completion time where supported
        - Fastest completion
        - Longest completion
        - Comparison with previous period
    
    
        1.4 TARGET-BASED PERFORMANCE
        ----------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures actual achievement against a defined target.
    
        Examples:
        - Number of tasks expected
        - Tasks completed
        - Daily target
        - Weekly target
        - Monthly target
        - Role-specific operational target
    
        Logic:
        - Retrieve the target applicable to the selected employee, role, team, or period.
        - Determine actual achieved value.
        - Compare actual achievement against target.
        - Calculate achievement percentage.
    
        Target Achievement =
        (Actual achievement / Defined target) × 100
    
        Handling:
        - Targets should be period-specific.
        - Targets should be role-aware where different roles have different expectations.
        - Changes to targets should preserve their historical versions.
        - Reports should indicate the target used for the calculation.
        - A target of zero must not produce an invalid division result.
        - Where no target exists, show "Target Not Configured" instead of assuming zero.
    
        Output:
        - Target
        - Actual
        - Achievement %
        - Variance from Target
        - Above / On Target / Below Target
    
    
        1.5 EMPLOYEE-SPECIFIC PERFORMANCE
        ----------------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Provides an individual employee performance view.
    
        Logic:
        - Filter all permitted task, attendance, activity, and performance records for the
          selected employee.
        - Combine relevant indicators.
        - Show performance over the selected period.
        - Compare the employee with their own historical performance.
        - Where organizational policy permits, compare against applicable team or role
          benchmarks.
        - Do not expose information outside the viewer's authorized scope.
    
        Possible Indicators:
        - Task completion rate
        - On-time completion
        - Average completion time
        - Target achievement
        - Overdue task count
        - Reassignment frequency
        - Blocker frequency
        - Work activity consistency
        - Attendance indicators
    
    
        1.6 MANAGER-SPECIFIC PERFORMANCE
        --------------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures managerial effectiveness based on team-level operational outcomes.
    
        Logic:
        - Aggregate performance of employees under the manager's authorized scope.
        - Measure task allocation effectiveness.
        - Measure overdue and blocked work.
        - Measure review and approval activity.
        - Measure resolution of employee issues.
        - Measure team target achievement.
        - Compare current team performance with previous periods.
        - Do not evaluate the manager solely from employee task completion count.
    
        Possible Indicators:
        - Team completion rate
        - Team on-time completion
        - Overdue workload
        - Review turnaround time
        - Reassignment frequency
        - Issue resolution time
        - Team target achievement
        - Team workload balance

    7.3 Performance Tiers & Monthly Rewards
    Tier
    Score Range
    Reward & Recognition
    Gold Tier
    90 – 100
    “Star Performer of the Month” digital badge, cash spot bonus / voucher, spotlight on announcement board, counts as 1 month toward 3-month award.
    Silver Tier
    80 – 89
    “High Achiever” commendation in team standup, full quarterly bonus eligibility.
    Bronze Tier
    70 – 79
    Meets baseline corporate expectations.
    Needs Focus
    < 70
    Triggers constructive 1-on-1 coaching with Lead Manager; automated review of workload or training gaps.
    7.4 The 3-Consecutive-Month Chairman’s Quarterly Excellence Award
    The Rule of Unwavering Consistency: Any employee can have a lucky month. True institutional excellence is demonstrated through sustained consistency.
    Continuous Qualification: An employee must achieve Gold Tier (score ≥ 90) in three consecutive calendar months (e.g., October, November, December).
    The Reset Covenant: If an employee achieves Gold in Month 1 and Month 2, but dips to Silver (85) in Month 3, the consecutive streak counter resets to zero.
    Chairman Cockpit Shortlist: Upon completing the third consecutive Gold month, the system automatically generates a Chairman Quarterly Honours Dossier presenting the 90-day activity summary.
    Executive Commendation: The Chairman reviews the dossier and signs off on the award.
    
    Quarterly Honours Package:
    Substantial Cash Incentive dispatched alongside the quarterly payroll.
    Formal Chairman’s Trophy & Framed Commendation, presented publicly during CIRIL’s Annual or Zonal Conference.
    Permanent Gold Star Profile Medallion displayed alongside the employee’s name across all system modules.
    Fast-Track Career Progression: Serves as the primary prerequisite for promotions.
    7.5 Role-Specific KPI Customisation
    To ensure fairness across diverse functional departments, the scoring engine tailors metrics by role:
    BDE (Bhavani): Outreach volume (calls, emails, WhatsApp sequences), prospect pipeline conversions, follow-up adherence.
    Social Media Manager (Harshitha): Content calendar adherence, market report proofreading timeliness, campaign delivery.
    Graphic Designer: Creative turnaround speed, version iteration efficiency (≤2 revision cycles), conference collateral print readiness.
    Lead Manager (Manasa): Team SLA adherence, royalty calculation accuracy, workload balance health.
    7.6 Anti-Burnout Safeguards
    No Midnight Heroics Required: Points are capped at standard shift durations. Working 14-hour days does not grant extra points.
    Transparent Dispute Resolution: If an employee feels their score was unfairly affected by external blockers, they can submit a 1-click Score Review Request to the Senior Manager.
    Open Self-Inspection: Employees can click on any score component to view the exact math and underlying records.

        1.7 TEAM PERFORMANCE
        ---------------------
    
        Definition:
        Provides an aggregated performance view for a team.
    
        Logic:
        - Group employee records by team.
        - Aggregate task and performance indicators.
        - Show distribution rather than only a single team average.
        - Identify whether poor team results originate from a few employees or are broadly
          distributed.
        - Allow drill-down:
          Team → Employee → Task → Activity / Evidence
    
    
        1.8 ROLE-BASED PERFORMANCE
        --------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures performance according to the work type and expectations applicable to
        a specific role.
    
        Logic:
        - Identify the user's authorized role/designation.
        - Apply the performance metrics configured for that role.
        - Avoid forcing identical KPIs onto roles with fundamentally different work.
        - Preserve a common reporting structure while allowing role-specific measures.
    
        Example:
        A BDE may be measured using lead/task outcomes,
        while a Graphic Designer may be measured using creative-task completion,
        revision cycles, submission quality, and deadline adherence.
    
    
        2. TASK & WORKLOAD ANALYTICS
        ============================
    
        FUNCTION
        --------
        Task and Workload Analytics analyzes how work is distributed, how much work is
        pending, where bottlenecks exist, who is overloaded, and how effectively assigned
        work is progressing.
    
        The layer should distinguish between:
        - Task quantity
        - Task status
        - Workload
        - Work progress
        - Task ownership
        - Reassignment history
    
    
        2.1 TASK DISTRIBUTION
        ---------------------
    
        Definition:
        Shows how tasks are distributed across employees, teams, managers, roles,
        priorities, and statuses.
    
        Logic:
        - Group tasks by the selected dimension.
        - Count tasks in each group.
        - Allow filtering by:
          - Date
          - Employee
          - Manager
          - Team
          - Role
          - Priority
          - Status
        - Show both task count and percentage of total.
        - Permit drill-down into the underlying task list.
    
        Example:
        Team A = 40%
        Team B = 35%
        Team C = 25%
    
    
        2.2 PENDING TASK ANALYSIS
        -------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures work that remains incomplete.
    
        Logic:
        - Identify tasks that are not in a final completed/closed state.
        - Separate normal active work from exceptions such as overdue or blocked work.
        - Group pending tasks by employee, team, priority, age, and deadline.
        - Highlight long-pending tasks.
        - Allow drill-down to task details.
    
    
        2.3 OVERDUE TASK ANALYSIS
        -------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Identifies tasks whose applicable deadline has passed without completion.
    
        Logic:
        - Compare current/reporting timestamp with the current authorized task deadline.
        - If the task remains incomplete after the deadline, classify it as Overdue.
        - Overdue tasks should not be treated merely as ordinary pending work.
        - Record:
          - Overdue date
          - Duration overdue
          - Priority
          - Current owner
          - Manager
          - Whether the task was reassigned
          - Whether a manager intervention occurred
        - Sort or classify overdue tasks by severity.
        - Provide a direct path from analytics to manager intervention.
    
    
        2.4 REASSIGNMENT FREQUENCY
        --------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures how frequently tasks are reassigned.
    
        Logic:
        - Count reassignment events from the task history.
        - Do not count a reassigned task as multiple tasks.
        - Count each actual ownership-transfer event separately.
        - Calculate:
          - Total reassignment events
          - Number of tasks reassigned
          - Reassignments per task
          - Reassignment by employee/team/manager
        - Identify repeated reassignment patterns.
        - Preserve the reason and history of every reassignment.
    
        This can identify:
        - Capacity problems
        - Assignment problems
        - Unclear ownership
        - Workflow problems
        - Repeated operational intervention
    
    
        2.5 PARTICULAR-EMPLOYEE WORKLOAD
        -------------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures the amount and intensity of work assigned to a specific employee.
    
        Logic:
        - Retrieve all tasks currently assigned to the employee.
        - Categorize by:
          - Active
          - Pending
          - Overdue
          - High priority
          - Blocked
          - Waiting for review
        - Consider deadlines and task priority rather than simply counting tasks.
        - Show current workload and historical workload.
        - Compare current workload against the employee's configured/expected capacity,
          where such capacity information exists.
    
        Possible Output:
        Employee
        Current Tasks
        High Priority Tasks
        Overdue Tasks
        Blocked Tasks
        Upcoming Deadlines
        Workload Level
    
    
        2.6 TEAM WORKLOAD
        -----------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures the distribution of active work across the team.
    
        Logic:
        - Aggregate employee workload records.
        - Compare workload across team members.
        - Identify concentration of work on individual employees.
        - Identify employees with significantly lower or higher active workload.
        - Show task volume alongside deadline pressure.
        - Provide a manager drill-down from:
          Team → Employee → Tasks
    
    
        2.7 WORKLOAD BALANCE
        --------------------
    
        Definition:
        Determines whether assigned work is reasonably distributed.
    
        Logic:
        - Calculate workload indicators for all applicable employees.
        - Compare workload against configured capacity or expected workload.
        - Classify employees as:
          - Normal
          - High
          - Overloaded
          where the actual thresholds are configurable.
        - Generate management attention when workload becomes materially unbalanced.
        - Do not automatically reassign work solely because an analytical threshold is
          reached; analytics should identify the condition, while authorized management
          actions perform the reassignment.
    
    
        2.8 UPCOMING WORKLOAD
        ---------------------
    
        Definition:
        Predicts near-term workload pressure from tasks whose deadlines are approaching.
    
        Logic:
        - Identify upcoming deadlines.
        - Group tasks by employee/team.
        - Consider task priority and due-date proximity.
        - Detect concentration of deadlines within a short period.
        - Flag employees likely to become overloaded.
        - Present this as an operational warning rather than a confirmed failure.
    
    
        3. ATTENDANCE ANALYTICS
        =======================
    
        FUNCTION
        --------
        Attendance Analytics analyzes attendance records, working hours, late arrivals,
        absences, availability, and workforce attendance patterns.
    
        Attendance and Availability must remain separate concepts.
    
        Attendance answers:
        "Was the employee present / working?"
    
        Availability answers:
        "Can the employee currently accept or handle work?"
    
    
        3.1 LATE ARRIVAL TRENDS
        -----------------------
    
        Definition:
        Identifies patterns in late arrivals over time.
    
        Logic:
        - Retrieve attendance check-in records.
        - Determine whether each arrival was on time or late using the organization's
          configured working schedule and applicable grace period.
        - Count late events by:
          - Employee
          - Team
          - Role
          - Date
          - Week
          - Month
        - Show trend over time.
        - Distinguish occasional late arrivals from repeated patterns.
        - Link back to attendance records for verification.
    
    
        3.2 ABSENCE TRENDS
        ------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures absence patterns across employees and teams.
    
        Logic:
        - Identify attendance records classified as absent.
        - Group by employee, team, role, and reporting period.
        - Distinguish approved leave from unexplained/flagged absence where the system
          contains that information.
        - Show frequency and trend.
        - Do not treat approved leave automatically as poor performance.
    
    
        3.3 AVAILABILITY PATTERNS
        -------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures how employee availability changes throughout working periods.
    
        Logic:
        - Analyze recorded availability states such as:
          Available
          Busy
          In Meeting
          On Break
          In Training
          Do Not Disturb
          Working Remotely
          On Leave
          Absent
          Offline
        - Measure duration/frequency where such timestamps are available.
        - Identify recurring periods of low availability.
        - Compare availability with workload and task deadlines.
    
    
        3.4 WORKFORCE ATTENDANCE PATTERNS
        ----------------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Provides an aggregated view of workforce attendance behavior.
    
        Logic:
        - Aggregate attendance records across teams, roles, branches, or departments.
        - Identify:
          - Attendance rate
          - Late-arrival patterns
          - Absence patterns
          - Remote/off-site patterns where recorded
          - Working-hour trends
        - Compare current period with historical periods.
        - Identify recurring organization-level patterns.
    
    
        3.5 WORK HOURS ANALYSIS
        -----------------------
    
        Definition:
        Measures recorded working duration for employees and teams.
    
        Logic:
        - Use check-in and check-out records.
        - Account for recorded break periods where supported.
        - Calculate work duration according to the organization's configured attendance
          policy.
        - Show:
          - Total hours
          - Average hours
          - Daily hours
          - Team totals
        - Flag incomplete or inconsistent attendance records separately instead of
          silently treating them as zero hours.
    
    
        4. EXCEPTION / RISK ANALYSIS
        ============================
    
        FUNCTION
        --------
        Exception and Risk Analysis identifies operational conditions that require
        attention.
    
        It converts raw operational conditions into manageable warnings.
    
        The system should distinguish between:
        - Normal operation
        - Attention required
        - High-risk operational condition
    
        Risk classification should be rule-based and configurable unless a formal
        predictive model is implemented.
    
    
        4.1 OVERDUE TASK DETECTION
        --------------------------
    
        Logic:
        - Continuously or periodically evaluate active task deadlines.
        - Detect tasks whose deadline has passed without completion.
        - Calculate overdue duration.
        - Consider task priority.
        - Consider whether the task is blocked, awaiting review, or otherwise subject
          to a valid workflow condition.
        - Create an analytical exception where appropriate.
        - Notify or surface the exception to the authorized manager dashboard.
        - Track whether the exception was resolved, extended, reassigned, or escalated.
    
    
        4.2 WORKLOAD RISK
        -----------------
    
        Definition:
        Identifies situations where an employee/team may not have sufficient capacity
        to handle assigned work within expected deadlines.
    
        Logic:
        - Evaluate active task count.
        - Evaluate upcoming deadlines.
        - Evaluate priority.
        - Evaluate overdue tasks.
        - Evaluate blocked work.
        - Compare workload against configured capacity where available.
        - Combine the conditions into a workload-risk assessment.
    
        Example logic:
        High active workload
        +
        multiple approaching deadlines
        +
        existing overdue work
        =
        Higher workload risk
    
    
        4.3 REPEATED REASSIGNMENT RISK
        ------------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Detects when a task or employee is repeatedly involved in reassignment events.
    
        Logic:
        - Count reassignment events over a configurable period.
        - Determine whether the configured threshold has been exceeded.
        - Identify:
          - Task repeatedly reassigned
          - Employee frequently receiving reassigned tasks
          - Employee frequently losing assigned tasks
          - Team-level reassignment concentration
        - Record the reason wherever available.
        - Create a risk/attention indicator when the configured threshold is reached.
        - Do not interpret reassignment automatically as employee failure; the reason
          must remain available for management review.
    
    
        4.4 RISK CLASSIFICATION
        -----------------------
    
        The system can classify detected exceptions into:
    
        LOW
        ---
        Indicates a condition that should be monitored but does not currently require
        urgent intervention.
    
        MEDIUM
        ------
        Indicates a recurring or materially important condition that should receive
        manager attention.
    
        HIGH
        ----
        Indicates a condition that requires immediate managerial or authorized higher-level
        attention.
    
        Handling Logic:
        - Risk level is generated from configured business rules.
        - Rules may consider:
          - Severity
          - Frequency
          - Duration
          - Priority
          - Impact
          - Repeated occurrence
        - Risk thresholds should be configurable.
        - Every risk result should show why it was classified at that level.
        - Risk should be traceable to underlying operational records.
        - Risk status should be capable of moving through:
          Detected → Reviewed → Action Taken → Resolved
        - Resolved risks remain in historical records for trend analysis.
    
    
        4.5 PENDING REQUEST RISK
        ------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Identifies requests that remain undecided beyond the expected handling period.
    
        Examples:
        - Leave request
        - Permission request
        - Resource request
        - Task clarification request
    
        Logic:
        - Determine request creation time.
        - Determine expected decision/response period where configured.
        - Identify requests that remain pending beyond that period.
        - Group by request type, requester, manager, and age.
        - Surface delayed requests to the appropriate authority.
        - Preserve request history and decisions.
    
    
        4.6 UNRESOLVED BLOCKER ANALYSIS
        -------------------------------
    
        Definition:
        Identifies employee-reported issues/blockers that remain unresolved and may
        impact work completion.
    
        Logic:
        - Detect active blocker/issue records.
        - Measure blocker age.
        - Identify affected task.
        - Identify employee and responsible manager.
        - Determine severity.
        - Determine whether escalation has occurred.
        - Prioritize blockers based on severity, task priority, and duration.
        - Surface long-running blockers as management exceptions.
    
    
        4.7 COMBINED OPERATIONAL RISK
        -----------------------------
    
        The system may combine multiple independent exceptions to identify a larger
        operational concern.
    
        Example:
    
        Employee has:
        - High workload
        - Multiple approaching deadlines
        - Existing overdue task
        - Open blocker
    
        The analytical layer can combine those conditions into a higher-priority
        management attention item.
    
        Important:
        This does not automatically mean the employee is underperforming.
        The system identifies the operational condition and allows management to inspect
        the underlying causes.
    
    
        5. TREND & HISTORICAL ANALYSIS
        ==============================
    
        FUNCTION
        --------
        Historical analysis compares current operational performance with previous periods
        to identify improvement, deterioration, recurring patterns, and changes.
    
        Logic:
        - Store historical analytical snapshots or calculate from historical operational
          records.
        - Compare:
          - Current day vs previous day
          - Current week vs previous week
          - Current month vs previous month
          - Current period vs equivalent previous period
        - Show direction:
          - Improving
          - Stable
          - Declining
        - Allow drill-down into the records causing the trend.
    
    
        5.1 PERFORMANCE TRENDS
        ----------------------
    
        Analyze:
        - Completion rate over time
        - On-time completion over time
        - Target achievement over time
        - Average completion time over time

    const esc=s=>String(s).replace(/&/g,'&amp;').replace(/</g,'&lt;').replace(/>/g,'&gt;').replace(/"/g,'&quot;');
    const pages=[];let p,seq=0;
    function page(id,title,subtitle,h=1350){p={id,title,h,cells:[],nodes:[]};pages.push(p);box(title,40,25,1520,55,'navy','bar');box(subtitle,40,90,1520,45,'gray','note');if(id!=='overview')box('← OVERVIEW',1350,145,210,36,'purple','link','overview');return p;}
    function box(label,x,y,w=240,h=74,color='blue',kind='box',link,parent='1'){
     const id=p.id+'-n'+(++seq),cc=C[color];let style='whiteSpace=wrap;html=1;fontFamily=Arial;fontSize=14;spacing=10;strokeWidth=1.3;shadow=0;';
     style+=(kind==='decision'?'rhombus;':kind==='data'?'shape=cylinder3;size=12;':kind==='note'?'rounded=0;strokeOpacity=0;align=left;':kind==='bar'?'rounded=1;fontStyle=1;fontSize=19;':'rounded=1;arcSize=12;');
     style+=`fillColor=${cc[0]};strokeColor=${cc[1]};fontColor=${color==='navy'?'#FFFFFF':'#1B2935'};`;
     if(kind==='link')style+='fontStyle=1;dashed=1;';
     const attrs=`label="${esc(label.replace(/\n/g,'<br>'))}"${link?` link="data:page/id,${link}"`:''}`;
     p.cells.push(`<object id="${id}" ${attrs}><mxCell style="${style}" vertex="1" parent="${parent}"><mxGeometry x="${x}" y="${y}" width="${w}" height="${h}" as="geometry"/></mxCell></object>`);
     p.nodes.push({id,label,x,y,w,h,parent});return id;
    }
    function edge(a,b,label='',color='gray',style=''){const id=p.id+'-e'+(++seq);p.cells.push(`<mxCell id="${id}" value="${esc(label)}" style="edgeStyle=orthogonalEdgeStyle;rounded=1;html=1;endArrow=block;endFill=1;fontFamily=Arial;fontSize=12;labelBackgroundColor=#FFFFFF;strokeColor=${C[color][1]};strokeWidth=1.4;${style}" edge="1" parent="1" source="${a}" target="${b}"><mxGeometry relative="1" as="geometry"/></mxCell>`);}
    function chain(nodes,color='gray'){for(let i=1;i<nodes.length;i++)edge(nodes[i-1],nodes[i],'',color);return nodes;}
    function bar(text,y,color='navy'){return box(text,40,y,1520,40,color,'bar');}
    function flow(title,x,y,steps,color='blue',w=325,gap=112,links={}){box(title,x,y,w,46,color,'bar');return chain(steps.map((t,i)=>box(t,x+12,y+72+i*gap,w-24,78,color,links[i]?'link':'box',links[i])),color);}

    page('basis','14 • Source basis, refinements & implementation decisions','Reading guide • 01 overview; 02–03 entry and roles; 04–11 operations; 12–13 intelligence. This file is an operational design, not a claim that these IOTS features are implemented.',1510);
    const sourceText=[
    ['AUTHORITATIVE REQUIREMENTS','gpt_prompt.txt\nTen roles and supplied responsibilities; multi-role context; three layers; Chairman visibility; equal SA / IT platform privilege; business approval boundaries.'],
    ['BUSINESS FOUNDATION','ciril_crm_detail.pdf, 20 pages\nMember network, lead giver / receiver, variable brokerage shares, lease-day calculation, 7.5% royalty, negotiation, proposals, payment and research context.'],
    ['PRESERVED DESIGN','../Flowchart/Actual-FlowChart.drawio\nLogin / location / audit sequence; three architectural lanes; six Admin branches; eight Manager branches. Complete employee and analytics flows from Construct.txt.'],
    ['SUPPORTING MATERIAL','claude_version.docx\nTask revision, audit principles, attendance, reporting and recognition concepts. Explicit prompt roles override section 2.2. Fixed share, scoring and shift examples are not binding company policy.'],
    ['VISUAL FOUNDATION','referential-flowchart.jpeg\nDark start/end; blue administrative controls; orange management; green execution; purple reporting; rounded process boxes, decision diamonds and orthogonal connectors.'],
    ['CRM CROSS-CHECK','Local code found at CRM/Updated/public_html/public_html\nReviewed routes, lead / assignment / brokerage / royalty controllers, key models and SQL schema. The requested CRM/Update/public_html location is absent.']
    ];sourceText.forEach((a,i)=>box(a[0]+'\n\n'+a[1],60+(i%2)*780,210+Math.floor(i/2)*215,740,180,'gray'));
    bar('REFINEMENTS THAT COMPLETE THE FOUNDATION',890,'purple');
    box('Entry and permissions\nPreserve the original login flow; add active-account / no-role cases, unknown location, primary role and context switch. Enforce active-role scope on actions and reporting.',65,955,475,160,'blue');
    box('Work and accountability\nComplete submission / correction / approval loops; explicit owner and escalation follow-up; separate task state from activity; overdue is a flag; reassignment keeps one task identity.',562,955,475,160,'orange');
    box('Records and insight\nSeparate checkout from session end; preserve corrections and history; role-scoped reporting; rules explain risks; Chairman appraisal and three-month recognition review.',1060,955,475,160,'purple');

    note('Confirm approval and escalation matrix; working hours, grace periods and break rules; report schedules; appraisal template; recognition scoring; retention rules; designated financial authority; CRM linking / import mechanism.',1230,80);
    note('CRM caution: current users.roleId is a single role field; IOTS multi-role assignment is a new requirement. CRM royalityStatus updates lead_status to 8 but inserts status_id 7 into lead_statuses. Mapping needs reconciliation before integration.',1320,75);
    note('Historical chat_gpt_chats.pdf was not needed. Draw.io MCP is unavailable in this session; this artifact uses native editable Draw.io XML. Sources are local; no source document or CRM application was modified.',1405,70);

    fs.writeFileSync('final-version.drawio',xml);

## Activity

    file changes: Completed · 1 changes
    Add: C:\Users\ciril\OneDrive\Desktop\Manikanta-Files\Git\monitoring-portal\internal-operations-tracking-system\iots-app\documentation\worker\Codex\flowchart-source-notes.md
    # CIRIL IOTS flowchart
    
    Open **final-version.drawio** in Draw.io. Start with page 01, then follow the dashed linked boxes or use the page tabs. The editable file contains 14 pages. A PDF and overview PNG are provided for presentation.
    
    ## Page guide
    
    | Page | Coverage |
    |---|---|
    | 01 | Chairman-friendly overview and the three foundational layers |
    | 02 | Credentials, active account, location, login audit and role access |
    | 03 | Ten roles within the three functional lanes; workspace switching |
    | 04 | Six administrative branches and governance boundaries |
    | 05 | Assignment, execution, evidence, review, corrections and closure |
    | 06 | Overdue intervention, reassignment, blockers and escalation |
    | 07 | Attendance, availability, requests, communication, profile and security |
    | 08 | IT monitoring, incidents, deployment records and platform governance |
    | 09 | General, Senior and Lead Manager responsibilities and shared controls |
    | 10 | BDE membership work, CRM commercial milestones and royalty follow-up |
    | 11 | Creative content, market reports, Office calls and event materials |
    | 12 | Metrics, trends, risks, reporting, scope and evidence drill-down |
    | 13 | Chairman oversight, appraisal exports, announcements and recognition |
    | 14 | Sources, design refinements and unresolved policy choices |
    
    ## Source precedence and review
    
    1. **gpt_prompt.txt:** authoritative IOTS scope and explicit role responsibilities. Its ten-role structure supersedes the eleven-role supporting document.
    2. **ciril_crm_detail.pdf:** business foundation. All 20 pages were extracted and reviewed; a few decorative glyphs were not recovered by text extraction.
    3. **../Flowchart/Actual-FlowChart.drawio:** original architecture and branches. Both existing pages were inspected through their diagram structure and labels.
    4. **Construct.txt:** functional scope for administrative, managerial, employee and analytical work.
    5. **claude_version.docx:** supporting operational concepts. Its role descriptions, numerical examples and proposed policies were not treated as stronger evidence than the prompt or company source.
    6. **referential-flowchart.jpeg:** inspected visually for colours, flow orientation and shape usage.
    
    The historical **chat_gpt_chats.pdf** was not needed.
    
    The stated `CRM/Update/public_html` path was absent. The available code was found at `C:/Users/ciril/OneDrive/Desktop/CRM/Updated/public_html/public_html`. Review focused on routes, lead assignment, brokerage and royalty controllers, relevant models, and schema definitions in `C:/Users/ciril/OneDrive/Desktop/CRM/cirilcrm_ciril.sql`. This was a domain review for the operational diagram, not an exhaustive application audit. No application code or source documents were changed.
    
    ## Corrections grounded in the sources
    
    - **Brokerage split varies by deal.** `Leads/BrokerageController.php::getBrokerage` reads giver and receiver shares, including custom percentages. The supporting document's fixed 30/70 split is not a universal rule.
    - **Lease brokerage varies with agreed days.** The code calculates rounded monthly rent value × agreed lease days / 30; sale brokerage is rounded 1% of transaction value.
    - **Royalty is calculated per party.** The code rounds 7.5% of the rounded giver share plus 7.5% of the rounded receiver share. This is approximately 7.5% of total brokerage when the agreed shares total 100%, subject to rounding.
    - **Payment confirmation is a separate event.** Commercial closure, acceptance of brokerage and confirmation of royalty payment have separate records and responsibilities. Completing an internal IOTS follow-up does not itself settle a CRM deal.
    - **Platform authority differs from business authority.** Super Admin and IT Analyst retain equal platform privileges; commercial, leave and deliverable approvals still require an explicit business mandate.
    - **CRM roles are not the IOTS role model.** The current `users.roleId` field describes one role per CRM user. Multiple IOTS roles require a separate design; they are not claimed to exist in the current schema.
    - **CRM integration must reconcile status history.** The reviewed `RoyaltyController::royalityStatus` sets `leads.lead_status` to 8 and `brokerage.royality` to 1, but inserts `status_id` 7 into `lead_statuses`. The diagram uses business milestones rather than presenting those numeric values as a reliable common state model.
    
    ## Design choices still requiring CIRIL policy
    
    These do not prevent reviewing the operational design, but need decisions before implementation:
    
    - Approval and escalation matrix, including business payment confirmation authority and delegated Admin interventions.
    - Actual shifts, grace periods, break accounting, attendance corrections and remote-work approval rules.
    - Reporting cadence, recipient rules and the exact Chairman appraisal export template.
    - Recognition weights, qualifying thresholds, incentives and period definitions. Three consecutive qualifying months and Chairman sign-off are included; the supporting document's specific score and reward amounts remain proposals.
    - CRM linking/import/integration mechanism, data reconciliation, retention and export configuration.
    
    The design recommends explicit checkout, separate unknown-location handling, active-account checks, per-action permission checks and immutable audit records. These are design requirements and refinements, not assertions that the supplied CRM already implements them.
    
    ## Artifact validation
    
    The file was parsed as XML and checked for unique IDs within each page, valid connector endpoints, edge geometry and valid internal page links. Draw.io was used to render the artifact for visual review. The Draw.io MCP tool was unavailable; native editable XML was authored using the installed Draw.io skill. The desktop version did not support the optional `libavoid` layout preset, so the supplied layout uses explicit positions and orthogonal connectors.
